(49)

for each local chart
\phi:\mathbbm{R}^{d}\rightarrow M
centered at
p\in M
. Hence, one may also think of the infinitesimal neighborhood at a point, up to isomorphism, as an infinitesimal disk attached to it via a local chart.
23
23

23

This latter – up to isomorphism – point of view is taken as a definition of infinitesimal neighborhoods in manifolds by
[
KS17
]
.

We may now generalize the interpretation of jets
j^{k}_{p}f
of any bundle
F\in M
over
M
as sections over infinitesimal neighborhoods
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\hookrightarrow M
.

Lemma 2.37

(
Jets as sections over infinitesimal neighborhoods).

Let
F\rightarrow M
be a fiber bundle over an
d
-dimensional manifold
M\in\mathrm{Man}
. Then:

(i)

The set of
k
-jets
J^{k}_{p}(F)
of sections at a point
p\in M
are in 1-1 correspondence with sections over the
k
-order infinitesimal neighborhood of
p\in M
, i.e., maps
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\rightarrow F
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\mathrlap{\,.}}

(ii)

Furthermore, for any choice of local chart
\phi:\mathbbm{R}^{d}\hookrightarrow U_{p}\subset M
centered at
p\in M
, they are in 1-1 correspondence with maps
\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow F
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{M\mathrlap{\,.}}

Proof.

Fix a local chart
\phi:\mathbbm{R}^{d}\rightarrow U_{p}\subset M
centered at p. A section
\sigma
of F over
\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\iota_{0}}}\mathbbm{R}^{d}\xrightarrow{\phi}M
fits in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{M\,.}

Pasting the commutative diagram (
49
) given by the isomorphism
j^{k}\phi:\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)
, we get

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{j^{k}\phi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which commutes since the internal diagrams do. Since
j^{k}\phi
is an isomorphism, we may define
\sigma\circ(j^{k}\phi)^{-1}:\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\rightarrow F
, which forms a section of F over the infinitesimal
neighborhood at
p
, since

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma\circ(j^{k}\phi)^{-1}}
\textstyle{M}

commutes by the previous diagram. Similarly, any section a section
\tilde{\sigma}
over the infinitesimal neighborhood
gives a section over
\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\iota_{0}}}\mathbbm{R}^{d}\xrightarrow{\phi}M
defined by
\tilde{\sigma}\circ j^{k}\phi
.
This proves the
(ii)
bijection of the statement.

Thus, it remains to prove the correspondence with jets of sections
J^{k}_{p}(F)
of
F\rightarrow M
in a local chart of
\tilde{\phi}:\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}\xrightarrow{~}\ F|_{U_{p}}
, compatible with the chart on the base
\phi:\mathbbm{R}^{d}\rightarrow M
.
Denote the corresponding coordinates functions on
\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}
by
\{x^{i},y^{j}\}
with
i=1,\cdots,d
and
j=1,\cdots,m
respectively.
By Hadamard’s Lemma
2.6
, a map
\sigma:\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow F|_{Up}\xrightarrow{\phi^{-1}}\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}

is completely determined, dually by the image of the linear coordinate functions
\{x^{i},y^{j}\}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})
.
The commutativity of the diagram of a section
\sigma:\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow F|_{Up}\xrightarrow{\phi^{-1}}\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}
over
\mathbbm{R}^{d}

then fixes the dual map to be of the form

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}:\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\big)

\displaystyle x^{i}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\epsilon^{i}

\displaystyle y^{j}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;c^{j}_{i}\cdot\epsilon^{i}+c^{j}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\cdot\epsilon^{i_{1}}\epsilon^{i_{2}}+\cdots+c^{j}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{d}}\cdot\epsilon^{i_{1}}\cdots\epsilon^{i_{k}}\,,

for some collection of real numbers
\big\{c^{j}_{i},\,c^{j}_{i_{1}i_{2}},\,\cdots,\,c^{j}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}\big\}
for
j=1,\cdots,m
,
i=1,\cdots,d
, symmetric under exchange of indices. Note that we have tacitly assumed, without loss of generality, that the
chart on
F|_{U_{p}}
is further centered around the image of the point
\tilde{\pi}:*\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow F
in the fiber
\pi^{-1}(p)
. Such a map polynomially extends along
\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\iota_{0}}}\mathbbm{R}^{d}
to a map
given dually by

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}^{*}:\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{d})

\displaystyle x^{i}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;x^{i}

\displaystyle y^{j}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;c^{j}_{i}\cdot x^{i}+c^{j}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\cdot x^{i_{1}}x^{i_{2}}+\cdots+c^{j}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{d}}\cdot x^{i_{1}}\cdots x^{i_{k}}\,,

or explicitly in the manifold picture,

\displaystyle\tilde{\sigma}:\mathbbm{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}

\displaystyle\tilde{x}=\tilde{x}^{i}\cdot e_{i}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\tilde{x}^{i}\cdot e_{i}\,,\;c^{j}_{i}\cdot\tilde{x}^{i}\cdot e_{j}+\cdots+c^{j}_{i_{1},\cdots i_{d}}\cdot\tilde{x}^{i_{1}}\cdots\tilde{x}^{i_{k}}\cdot e_{j}\big)\,,

where
{\tilde{x}^{i}}
denote the
standard coordinates of a point
\tilde{x}\in\mathbbm{R}^{d}
and
\{e_{i},e_{j}\}
are the standard basis vectors
for
\mathbbm{R}^{d}
and
\mathbbm{R}^{m}
respectively. By construction,
\tilde{\sigma}
fits in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\quad\mathbbm{R}^{d}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\phi}}
\textstyle{F|_{U_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{d}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\sigma}}
\textstyle{U_{p}\,,}

and so the map
\tilde{\phi}\circ\tilde{\sigma}\circ\tilde{\phi}:U_{p}\rightarrow F
defines a local section of
F
,
whose jet class is completely determined by
\sigma:\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow F
, by definition. Conversely, the representative
of any jet class
j^{k}\tilde{\sigma}\in J^{k}_{p}(F)
, defines a map
\mathbbm{D}^{d}(k)\rightarrow F|_{U_{p}}
as its (formal) Taylor
expansion in the given compatible trivialization as above.
∎

We have thus characterized the set of jets of sections
J^{k}_{p}(F)
of a bundle at a point
p\in M
, as sections over the
infinitesimal neighborhood
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(k)\hookrightarrow M
, hence dispensing of
almost
all reference to local charts.
Of course, the original definition (Def.
2.36
) of
J^{k}_{p}(M)
still implicitly employs local charts.

Working within thickened smooth sets,
there is a completely natural and intuitive definition of infinitesimal neighborhoods in any thickened smooth set, which does not refer
to any choice of local chart, and hence actually allows one to define jets of sections of any map between thickened smooth sets.
To that end, let us first declare following definition.

Definition 2.38

(
Infinitesimal Shape).

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
be a thickened smooth set. The
infinitesimal shape
,
or
de Rham shape
,
\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
of
\mathcal{F}
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}\;:\;\mathrm{ThCartSp}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}

(50)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})

for any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{ThCartSp}
.

In words, the infinitesimal shape of a space
\mathcal{F}
has the same
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots but no infinitesimal plots. Notice there is a natural smooth map

\displaystyle\eta_{\mathcal{F}}\;:\;\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}

(51)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}

(52)

which simply sends every
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot to its pullback
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}:=\iota^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}

along the canonical embedding
\iota:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\{*\}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
.
Thinking of
(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
-plots in
\mathcal{F}
as smooth
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots with an extra thickening given by an infinitesimal extension,
then
\eta_{\mathcal{F}}
exhibits
\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}
as the space obtained by identifying / collapsing the infinitesimal extension,
since any
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\psi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
with
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}=\psi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}
map to precisely the
same
(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
-plot in
\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}
. Colloquially, one says that the operation
\mathfrak{J}
identifies ‘infinitesimal neighbors’.
It is immediate to see that the infinitesimal shape forms a functor
24
24

24

Incidentally, there exists a right adjoint
\mathfrak{R}:\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\rightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
called reduction, which reduces a thickened smooth set
to its underlying body smooth set, in a precise sense, rather than
reducing its plots. These, in fact, arise from a deeper structure involving a quadruple of adjoints, termed “
Differential Cohesion
”. For full details on these aspects, see
[
Sc13a
]
[
KS17
]
.

\mathfrak{J}\;:\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,,

with the corresponding maps
\eta_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}
comprising a natural transformation

\eta\;:\;\mathrm{id}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}\,.

With the infinitesimal shape at hand, we may form an intuitive thickened smooth set that deserves the name of the “
\infty
-order infinitesimal
neighborhood” of a point
p:{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

Definition 2.39

(
Synthetic infinitesimal neighborhood).

Let
p:*\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a point in a thickened smooth set
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
. The
(synthetic) infinitesimal neighborhood

\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}
of
\mathcal{F}
at
p
is defined as the subspace given by the pullback

\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}\,.}

Explicitly, this means that the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of
\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},{*}\big)\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\times{*}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\big|\;\eta_{\mathcal{F}}(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon})=p(*)\Big\}

\displaystyle\cong\Big\{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\big|\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}=\mathrm{const}_{p}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\Big\}\,.

(53)

Said otherwise, the plots of
\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}
are constant onto the point
p\in\mathcal{F}(*)
along their purely finite directions,
but free to probe all infinitesimally thickened directions inside
\mathcal{F}
at p. The left vertical map of the pullback defines
a natural embedding

\iota_{p}:\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\,,

realizing the infinitesimal neighborhood as a thickened (smooth) subspace of
\mathcal{F}
. Thus, the
synthetic
infinitesimal neighborhood captures precisely the intuitive properties
of a reasonable notion of a “
\infty
-order infinitesimal neighborhood”, applicable for any ambient thickened smooth set. Of course, this definition recovers
that the traditional notion of an infinitesimal neighborhood of a finite-dimensional manifold (Def.
2.36
).

Lemma 2.40

(
Synthetic infinitesimal neighborhood at point in manifold).

Let
y(M)\in\mathrm{ThickenedSmoothSet}
be a smooth
d
-dimensional manifold viewed as a thickened smooth set and
p:{*}\rightarrow y(M)
a
point in
M
. There is a canonical isomorphism

\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\cong y\big(\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(\infty)\big)\,

between the synthetic and the traditional
\infty
-order infinitesimal neighborhoods at
p\in M
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that their plots are in natural bijection. We prove this for the cases of
\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
-plots, with the more general cases of thickened probes
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
following similarly via any subspace identification of from Lem.
2.8
. On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)

\displaystyle\cong\Big\{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in y(M)\big((\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\;\big|\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}=\mathrm{const}_{p}\in y(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\Big\}

\displaystyle\cong\Big\{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{op}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l),M\big)\;\big|\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}=\mathrm{const}_{p}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Man}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k},M)\Big\}\,.

Now any algebra morphism
C^{\infty}(M)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
corresponds precisely to a higher order differential operator
X_{p}:C^{\infty}(M)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
relative to the evaluation pullback algebra map
p:C^{\infty}(M)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, given by “constant evaluation” at
p\in M
(cf. proof of Lem.
2.22
). This means that the value of the differential operator
X_{p}
depends only on germs of functions, hence factors through any local chart subalgebra

X_{p}\,:\,C^{\infty}(M)\xlongrightarrow{\iota^{*}_{U_{p}}}C^{\infty}(U_{p})\xlongrightarrow{\sim}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{d})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})

centered at
p\in U_{p}\hookrightarrow M
.

In other words, any such plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
factors through a local chart of
M

\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{d}\xrightarrow{\sim}U_{p}\hookrightarrow M\,,

and so is determined by a map of thickened Cartesian spaces
\tilde{\phi}^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l),\,\mathbbm{R}^{d}\big)
. By Prop.
4.6
, these are dually
determined by the image of the linear coordinates
\{x^{i}\}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{d})

x^{i}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;c^{i}_{a}\epsilon^{a}+c^{i}_{ab}\cdot\epsilon^{a}\epsilon^{b}+\cdots+c^{i}_{a_{1}\cdots a_{l}}\cdot\epsilon^{a_{1}}\cdots\epsilon^{a_{l}}\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big),

with
\{c^{i}_{a},\cdots c^{i}_{a_{1}\cdots a_{l}}\}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, with no term valued purely in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
since this vanishes by the constancy assumtion along
p:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\rightarrow M
(and the fact that the chart is chosen to be centered at
p\in M
).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle y\big(\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(\infty)\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l))

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\big(J^{\infty}_{p}M\,,\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l))\big)\,,

and in the given local chart centered p,

J^{\infty}_{p}M\cong\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D}^{d}(\infty))\cong\mathbbm{R}[[\epsilon^{1},\cdots,\epsilon^{d}]]
by (
48
),
so that an algebra map
J^{\infty}_{p}M\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
is again completely determined by the image of the
generators
\{\epsilon^{i}\}
, as

\epsilon^{i}\;\;\longmapsto\;\;c^{i}_{a}\epsilon^{a}+c^{i}_{ab}\cdot\epsilon^{a}\epsilon^{b}+\cdots+c^{i}_{a_{1}\cdots a_{l}}\cdot\epsilon^{a_{1}}\cdots\epsilon^{a_{l}}\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\,.

Thus, we see there is a canonical correspondence between plots in
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}
and those in

y\big(\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(\infty)\big)
, which is moreover manifestly functorial under pullbacks of probe spaces.
∎

Application: Formalizing perturbative field theory.

In the traditional practice of perturbative field theory, one commonly considers the perturbation theory of local functionals of the fields (action functionals, currents, Euler–Lagrange operators etc.) by syntactically “Taylor expanding” in a “small perturbation h” around a fixed field configuration
\phi\in\mathcal{F}
. Here, we motivate that the restriction of said local functionals to infinitesimal neighborhoods around fixed field configurations

\mathbbm{D}_{\phi,\mathcal{F}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}

ought to rigorously encode this traditional notion of perturbation theory around a fixed field configuration
\phi
. We leave the explicit proof of this result in the infinite-dimensional setting of field theory for future work, as it will require much more technical effort than the following simple motivating example.

To witness the plausibility of this, we consider the toy-model case of 0-dimensional field theory, that is, with field bundle
M\rightarrow*
over a 0-dimensional (point) space-time. In that case, the space of fields is
[*,M]\cong M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, with dynamics described by (the criticality of) a smooth map of manifolds
S:M\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
. Embedding the picture in thickened smooth sets, or equivalently simply in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{op}
in this finite-dimensional case, we may choose a field configuration
p\in M
(critical or not) and consider the restriction on the infinitesimal neighborhood around it

\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}(\infty)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\,.

Now notice that the (defining) function algebra on the infinite order infinitesimal disk
\mathbbm{D}^{d}(\infty)\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{d}
is further canonically identified with the (formal completion of) the symmetric algebra of the cotangent space
T_{0}^{*}\mathbbm{R}^{d}

\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D}^{d}(\infty)\big)\,:=\,\mathbbm{R}[[\epsilon^{1},\cdots,\epsilon^{d}]]\quad\cong\quad\mathbbm{R}[[\mathrm{d}x^{1},\cdots,\mathrm{d}x^{d}]]\,\cong\,\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(T^{*}_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{d})\,.

Under this identification, by the duality of
T^{*}_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{d}
and
T_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{d}
, the elements of this algebra may also be thought of as ‘formal polynomial maps’ on the tangent space
T_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{d}
at
0\in\mathbbm{R}^{d}
, i.e., formal polynomials

T_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{d}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,.

The same can be said for the (jet) function algebras of infinitesimal neighborhoods in any manifold
M
(Def.
2.36
), but the isomorphism to formal polynomials then depends on the choice of chart. Indeed, recall by (
48
) that a choice of chart gives an isomorphism

\displaystyle j^{\infty}\phi^{*}:J^{\infty}_{p}(M)

\displaystyle\;\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\;\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{d}(\infty)\big)\cong\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(T^{*}_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{m})

\displaystyle j^{k}_{p}f

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;j^{k}_{0}(f\circ\phi)=f\circ\phi(0)+\partial_{i}(f\circ\phi)(0)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{i}+\tfrac{1}{2}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(f\circ\phi)(0)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{i}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{j}+\cdots\,,

which upon pulling back
each
of cotangent vector generators
\{\mathrm{d}x^{i}\}
along the same chart
yields a composite algebra isomorphism

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{p}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}\phi^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(T^{*}_{p}M)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{d}(\infty)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(T^{*}_{0}\mathbbm{R}^{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,.}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{*}}

Thus any choice of local chart
\phi
, interprets the (canonical jet) function algebra on
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\hookrightarrow M
as formal power series on the tangent space

\displaystyle j^{\infty}_{p}f|_{\phi}\;\;:\;\;T_{p}M\;\;

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\;\mathbbm{R}

\displaystyle h^{i}\partial_{i}|_{p}

\displaystyle\longmapsto f\circ\phi(0)+\partial_{i}(f\circ\phi)(0)\cdot h^{i}+\partial_{i}\partial_{j}(f\circ\phi)(0)\cdot h^{i}h^{j}+\cdots.

Now, given an ‘action’ functional
S:M\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
(or analogously its differential
\mathrm{d}S:M\rightarrow T^{*}M
etc.),
we may restrict to the infinitesimal neighborhood around
p\in M

S|_{\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}}:\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\hookrightarrow M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,,

which, dually, corresponds canonically to the
\infty
-jet
j^{\infty}_{p}S\in J^{\infty}_{p}(M)
, via
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}),\,J^{\infty}_{p}(M)\big)\,\cong\,J^{\infty}_{p}(M)
. Thus, under a
choice
of coordinate chart around p, whereby
J^{\infty}_{p}M\cong_{\phi}\mathrm{Sym}^{\bullet}(T^{*}_{p}M)
via (
2.4
), this yields exactly
the corresponding formal Taylor expansion

Tayl_{p,\phi}(S)=S(p)+\sum_{|l|=0}^{\infty}\tfrac{1}{l!}\partial_{l}S(p)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{l}\,.

By duality, this may be seen as acting on tangent vectors
h=h^{i}\partial_{i}|_{p}\in T_{p}M
,
hence recovering precisely the formal expansion

``S(p+h)^{\prime\prime}\equiv\mathrm{Tayl}_{p,\phi}(S)(h):=S(p)+\partial_{i}S(p)\cdot h^{i}+\partial_{i}\partial_{j}S(p)\cdot h^{i}h^{j}+\cdots

in the “small perturbation”
h\in T_{p}M
, with respect to the given chart.

Remark 2.41

(
Equivalent perturbative field theories).

Formulated in this rigorous setting, it is immediate that a choice of different chart
\psi
gives an equivalent perturbative field theory, in the sense that there exists (by construction) an isomorphism of infinitesimal disks
j^{\infty}(\psi\circ\phi^{-1})=j^{\infty}\psi\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{-1}
intertwining the two restricted (i.e., perturbative) action functionals. In this sense, the abstract restriction to the infinitesimal neighborhood
S|_{\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}}:\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
encodes perturbative field theory in a coordinate-invariant manner.

Notice, combining Lem.
2.37
and Lem.
2.40
, it follows that the set of
\infty
-order jets
J^{\infty}_{p}(F)
of a fiber bundle
F\rightarrow M
is in bijection with sections over the synthetic infinitesimal neighborhood

\textstyle{y(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y(M)}

as maps of thickened smooth sets. Formulated as above, the notion of
\infty
-jets of sections may be immediately extended to arbitrary ‘bundles’ of
thickened smooth sets.

Definition 2.42

(
\infty
-jets of sections).

Let
\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a map of thickened smooth sets and
p:*\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
a point in
\mathcal{F}
. We define the set
J^{\infty}_{p}(\mathcal{G})
of ‘
\infty
-jets of sections at p’ as the set of sections of
\pi
over the infinitesimal neighborhood at
p
.
That is, morphisms
\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
of thickened smooth sets such that

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}}

commutes. Suggestively, we denote the collection of jets over all points by
J^{\infty}(\mathcal{G})(*):=\underset{p\in M}{\bigcup}J^{\infty}_{p}(\mathcal{G})
.

Remark 2.43

(
Finite order synthetic neighborhoods).

Finite order synthetic disks
\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}(k)
can also be defined, for each
k\in\mathbbm{N}
, again as a pullback construction by employing a ‘
k
-order
infinitesimal shape’ functor
\mathfrak{J}^{k}:\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\rightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
. Along the same lines, one may define
k
-jets of sections as sections over the

k
-order infinitesimal neighborhoods
\mathbbm{D}_{p,\mathcal{F}}(k)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
, which again recover those over finite-dimensional manifolds. We focus on the infinite-order infinitesimal neighborhoods as they are the ones of most direct relevance to
our field-theoretic perspective.

Having formulated now the notion of
\infty
-jets at a point purely in terms of maps in thickened smooth sets, there is a natural thickened smooth set structure on
J^{\infty}_{p}(F)

given as in (
2.3
), by considering
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-parametrized sections over
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}
.
This suggests that it is possible to further endow the full collection
\underset{p\in M}{\bigcup}J^{\infty}_{p}(F)
of jets over of fiber a
bundle
F\rightarrow M
with a thickened smooth structure, by working entirely within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
. This is indeed the case and in fact applies in the generality of arbitrary bundles in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
and their jets
(Def.
2.42
), but that requires some further natural constructions which we include in the Appendix
section

4.3
, since these are not necessary for the field theoretic purposes of this text.

Infinitesimal neighborhoods of submanifolds.

Let us instead step back into the more concrete setting of sections of finite-dimensional fiber bundles
F\rightarrow M
and their jets along embedded submanifolds, a notion that naturally arises in field theory when considering “initial conditions” of fields along a submanifold (see e.g.
[
Part I
, §6.3]
).
Viewing any point
p:*\hookrightarrow M
as an embedded submanifold, what we have shown in Lem.
2.37
and Lem.
2.40
may be phrased as follows: Sections of the jet bundle
J^{\infty}F\rightarrow M
over
p:*\hookrightarrow M
are precisely sections of the original bundle
F\rightarrow
over the infinitesimal neighborhood
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\hookrightarrow M

\displaystyle\left(\!\!\!\!\raisebox{18.0pt}{

40.8718pt\raise-15.84021pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.52084pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{p}}$}}}\kern 44.45082pt\raise-21.33327pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 80.45087pt\raise-21.33327pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\!\!\right)\,.

This suggests that a similar equivalent description for sections of the jet bundle over
any
embedded submanifold
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
should exist, for an appropriate notion of an “infinitesimal neighborhood around
\Sigma
in
M
”. However,
working within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, there is a natural generalization of Def.
2.39
to yield such a notion (cf. Def.
4.21
).

Definition 2.44

(
Synthetic infinitesimal neighborhood of submanifold).

Let
\iota_{\Sigma}:\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
be an embedding submanifold of
M
, viewed as thickened smooth sets. The (
\infty
-order)
synthetic infinitesimal neighborhood

\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}
of
\Sigma
in
M
is defined as the thickened smooth subspace given by the pullback

\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{M}\circ\iota_{\Sigma}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{M}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{J}M\,.}

Explicitly, this means that the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of
\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle=\Big\{\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)\,\in\,M(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\times\Sigma(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\big|\;\eta_{M}(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon})=\eta_{M}(\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon})\Big\}

\displaystyle\cong\Big\{\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)\,\in\,M(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\times\Sigma(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\big|\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}=\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon=0}\in\Sigma(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\Big\}\,.

In words, the plots of
\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}
are given by arbitrary plots
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}
in
\Sigma
, paired with plots
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
of
M
which agree with
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}
in their finite directions, but are free to probe
M
in all their infinitesimally thickened directions! Of course, this is precisely what one would intuitively expect from a notion of an infinitesimal neighborhood of a subspace
\Sigma
in
M
, with the left vertical map giving the expecting embedding

\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\,.

Naturally, working explicitly with this thickened smooth set would be greatly facilitated by showing it is representable (from the outside) by the (formal dual) of some algebra
\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}(\infty)\in\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
. This is much like the case of the point, that is an algebra canonically associated to the pair
(\Sigma,M)
with an appropriate nilpotency behavior “along normal directions of
\Sigma
in
M
”.

Example 2.45

(
Infinitesimal neighborhood of line in plane).

As motivation for the abstract definition of these algebras, let us consider the toy example of a fiber bundle
F\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}
over the plane and the canonical embedding along the
x
-axis
\iota:\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}
. In this situation, we intuitively know what should be the infinitesimal neighborhood of
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}
in
\mathbbm{R}^{2}
, namely the thickened Cartesian subspace

\mathbbm{D}_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x},\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}(k)}\,:=\,\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}\times\mathbbm{D}^{1}(k)\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbbm{R}^{2}

given dually by the projection (cf. Cor.
4.2
)

C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}\big)\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}\big)\big/\big(y^{k+1}\big)\,\cong\,C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(k)\big)\,.

Working precisely along the lines
25
25

25

The only difference being that the coefficients
\big\{c^{j}_{i},c^{j}_{i_{1}i_{2}},\cdots,c^{j}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}\big\}
appearing therein are now functions on
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}
, rather than constants.
of Lem.
2.37
then yields a canonical bijection between sections of the jet bundle
J^{k}F\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}
over
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}
and sections of
F\rightarrow M
over
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}\times\mathbbm{D}^{1}(k)\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}
. Moreover, working along the lines of Lem.
2.40
, one can show that this represent the
synthetic infinitesimal neighborhood of
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}
from Def.
2.44
, i.e.,

\mathbbm{D}_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x},\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}}\,\cong\,y\big(\mathbbm{D}_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x},\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}}(\infty)\big)\quad\in\quad\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Now notice that the latter algebra
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y})/(y^{k+1})\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(k)\big)
is also canonically identified with the algebra of
jets of functions on
\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}
along
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}

\displaystyle J^{k}_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}}(\mathbbm{R}^{2}):=\Big\{j^{k}_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}}f=[f]\,\big|\,f\sim f^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(M)\iff\partial_{|I|}f|_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}}=\partial_{|I|}f^{\prime}|_{\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{x}}\hskip 8.5359pt\forall k\geq I\geq 0\Big\}\,,

where the derivatives are only along the normal
\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{y}
-axis in
\mathbbm{R}^{2}_{x,y}
. This formulation generalizes directly to yield the appropriate notion for an infinitesimal neighborhood along arbitrary
embeddings
of submanifolds.

Definition 2.46

(
Infinitesimal neighborhoods of submanifold).

Let
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
be an embedded submanifold. For any
k\in\mathbbm{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,

(i)
the
k
-order
infinitesimal neighborhood around
\Sigma
in
M
, denoted by
\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}(k)
, is the formal dual of the jet algebra of
M
along
\Sigma

\displaystyle J^{k}_{\Sigma}(M):=\Big\{j^{k}_{p}f=[f]\,\big|\,f\sim f^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(M)\iff\partial_{|I|}f|_{\Sigma}=\partial_{|I|}f^{\prime}|_{\Sigma}\hskip 8.5359pt\forall k\geq I\geq 0\Big\}\quad\in\quad\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}\,,

with the partial derivatives ranging over
normal
derivatives w.r.t. (any) adapted slice chart
\mathbbm{R}^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{\sigma}\times\mathbbm{R}^{d-\sigma}
for
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
;

(ii)
the
\infty
-order neighborhood around
\Sigma
in M
is given by the formal dual of the colimit of
k
-order neighborhoods in
\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}
, or dually as the limit of
k
-order infinitesimal neighborhoods in
\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{op}

\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}(\infty)\,:=\,\lim_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}(k)\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}\,.

With this definition at hand, we have the following.

Proposition 2.47

(
Sections over infinitesimal neighborhoods of submanifolds).

Let
F\rightarrow M
be a fiber bundle over an
d
-dimensional manifold
M\in\mathrm{Man}
and
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
an embedding submanifold. Then:

(i)
There is a canonical isomorphism

\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}\cong y\big(\mathbbm{D}_{\Sigma,M}(\infty)\big)\,

between the synthetic and the traditional
\infty
-order infinitesimal neighborhoods around
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
.

(ii)

Sections of the jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F
over
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
are in 1-1 correspondence with sections of
F\rightarrow M
over the
\infty
-order infinitesimal neighborhood of
\Sigma
in
M
,

\left(\!\!\!\!\raisebox{18.0pt}{
\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 73.94412pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.54552pt\raise-10.79436pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.54552pt\raise-14.31096pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise-21.58873pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.69936pt\raise-5.5039pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}_{\Sigma}\phi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.94412pt\raise-3.56479pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise-21.58873pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.3804pt\raise-21.58873pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern Proof.

The proof of
\bf(i)
follows as that of Lem.
2.40
. The proof of
\bf(ii)
follows as that of Lem.
2.37
with minimal modifications, by working over (adapted) covers of slice charts for
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
and trivialization of
F\rightarrow M
and essentially carrying along the new dependence of the functions therein along
\Sigma
.
∎

This result finally puts into firm ground the intuitive notion that initial data of PDEs are literally certain
26
26

26

That is, those that factor through the prolongated shell of the corresponding differential operator
\mathcal{P}:=P\circ j^{\infty}
,
S^{\infty}_{P}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
, now formed as a thickened subspace within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
(cf.
[
Part I
, Defs. 5.16, 6.29]
).
sections over an infinitesimal neighborhood of a submanifold (see, e.g., the discussion around
[
Part I
, Def. 6.28]
).

3
Local
Lagrangian field theory

We have defined the very basic building blocks for local field theory as thickened smooth sets, namely, the field spaces
\mathcal{F}:=\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
and the infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F
etc., and recovered their tangent bundles via the synthetic tangent bundle construction
T=\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),-\big]
. All the constructions performed on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
(cf.
[
Part I
]
) that are relevant to field theory (cf. Rem.
3.5
) can now be interpreted as taking place in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, and we briefly review how this works in
section

3.1
.

More importantly, however, working in our thickened setting allows for a fully solid consideration of the differential geometry on the actual field space, and its product with spacetime
\mathcal{F}\times M
. That is achieved by showing how all relevant tangent bundles and the pushfoward maps of the prolongation
j^{\infty}
and prolongated evaluation
ev^{\infty}
, which have previously only been defined – at best – in an ad-hoc manner (cf.
[
Part I
]
), are now rigorously recovered as applications of the same
synthetic tangent functor
of thickened smooth sets.

Beyond putting these aspects of the theory on a fully solid ground, this bears the further fruit of recognizing the variational principle of local Lagrangian field theory, equivalently, as an intersection
of thickened smooth sets (Thm.
3.17
, Rem.
3.19
). This means that the on-shell field space is naturally endowed with the correct infinitesimal structure, whose
synthetic
tangent bundle rigorously recovers the (again ad-hoc) notion of “Jacobi fields” (Cor.
3.20
). Lastly, it offers a prospect for further rigorously formalizing perturbative field theory as
literally
the restriction to a (synthetic) infinitesimal neighborhood (Def.
2.39
) around a field configuration
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

3.1
Geometry of the infinite jet bundle

Horizontal splitting.
The discussion spelled out in

[
Part I
, §4.2]
mostly depends on universal properties of limits, and applies essentially verbatim in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, as smooth manifolds embed fully faithfully (Ex.
12
). Namely, there is a short exact sequence of thickened smooth sets

\displaystyle 0_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow VJ^{\infty}_{M}F{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}\,,

(66)

where
VJ^{\infty}_{M}F

is the vertical subbundle
[
Part I
, Def. 4.6]
, defined as the equalizer of
\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}:TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow TM
(see (
43
)) and
0_{M}\circ\pi_{M}\circ\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}:TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow TM
, or equivalently as the pullback

\textstyle{VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{M}}
\textstyle{TM\,.}

Yet, equivalently, there is the following useful characterization:

Lemma 3.1

(
Vertical jet bundle characterization).

The vertical subbundle of
TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
is canonically identified with the infinite jet bundle of
VF\rightarrow F\rightarrow M
, i.e.,

V(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\cong\;J^{\infty}_{M}(VF)\,,

as bundles of thickened smooth sets over
F
, and hence over
M
.

Proof.

The pullback characterization (
3.1
) along with the limit property from Lem.
2.34
implies that

\displaystyle VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong\lim_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}VJ^{n}_{M}F\,,

(71)

where each
VJ^{n}_{M}F\hookrightarrow TJ^{n}_{M}F
is traditional (finite-dimensional) vertical tangent bundle of the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
-order jet bundle. At the finite-dimensional manifold level, it is a standard fact that

J^{n}_{M}(VF)\cong V(J^{n}_{M}F)

as bundles of manifolds over
F
, canonically, as can be immediately seen in local coordinates (see e.g.

[
Sa02
, (6.8)]
)

\big(x^{\mu},\,u^{a}_{I},\,\dot{(u^{a}_{I})}\big)\longmapsto\big(x^{\mu},\,(u^{a})_{I},\,(\dot{u}^{a})_{I}\big)\,.

Carrying this isomorphism through the fully faithful embedding into
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
(Ex.
12
), it follows that

\displaystyle VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\;\cong\;\lim_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}VJ^{n}_{M}F\cong\lim_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}J^{n}_{M}VF=:J^{\infty}_{M}VF\,.

∎

At this point, we recall the canonical smooth pushforward maps of manifolds (
[
Part I
, (76)]
)

\displaystyle H^{n}\;:\;J^{n+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\;T(J^{n}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\big(\,j^{n+1}_{p}\phi,\,X_{p}\big)

\displaystyle\;{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\;\mathrm{d}\big(j^{n}\phi\big)_{p}(X_{p})

for each
n\in\mathbbm{N}
, where
\phi:U\subset M\rightarrow F
on the right-hand side is any representative local section of

j^{n+1}_{p}\phi
. These fit into the commutative diagram of smooth manifolds

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.05272pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-36.39772pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{n+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.05272pt\raise-13.62503pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67558pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi^{n+1}_{n}\times\mathrm{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-19.91675pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.32645pt\raise 5.5853pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{H^{n}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.24387pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.39781pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 98.24387pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T\big(J^{n}_{M}F\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.03099pt\raise-13.62503pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\pi^{n}_{n-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.03099pt\raise-17.75006pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-13.62503pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-33.05174pt\raise-27.25006pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{J^{n}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.84848pt\raise-21.13005pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.12001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{H^{n-1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.3979pt\raise-27.25006pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.39781pt\raise-27.25006pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 94.3979pt\raise-27.25006pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T\big(J^{n-1}_{M}F\big)\,,}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

which we interpret as a commutative diagram of thickened smooth sets via Ex.
12
. This is already sufficient to provide the splitting from
[
Part I
, Prop. 4.8]
, however now within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
.

Proposition 3.2

(
Splitting of infinite jet tangent bundle).

The family of smooth bundle maps
\big\{H^{n}:J^{n+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{n}_{M}F)\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}
determines a map of thickened smooth sets

\displaystyle H\;:\;\,J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\;\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\;T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

(78)

which splits the corresponding exact sequence (
66
), and hence induces a canonical isomorphism of vector-bundle-thickened smooth sets

\displaystyle TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\;\cong\;VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\,\oplus\,HJ^{\infty}_{M}F\,,

over
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, where the plots of
HJ^{\infty}_{M}F
are defined by the (plot-wise) image of the map (
78
).

Proof.

By the limit property
of
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)=\mathrm{lim}_{n}^{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}T(J^{n}_{M}F)
from Lem.
2.34
and commuting limits through the hom functor, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\,,\,T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\;\cong\;\mathrm{lim}_{n}^{\mathrm{Set}}\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\,,\,T(J^{n}_{M}F)\big)\,.

Hence a map
f:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
corresponds to a family of maps of thickened smooth sets

\big\{f^{n}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow T(J^{n}_{M}F)\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}
such that
\mathrm{d}\pi^{n}_{n-1}\circ f^{n}=f^{n-1}
, and vice-versa.
Pulling back the maps from (
3.1
) along the canonical projections
\pi_{n+1}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{n+1}_{M}F
, we get a family

(\pi_{n+1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{n}\;\;:\;\;J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{n+1}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T(J^{n}_{M}F)

which by the
commutativity of diagram (
3.1
) satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\pi^{n}_{n-1}\circ\big(\pi_{n+1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{n}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\pi^{n}_{n-1}\circ H^{n}\circ(\pi_{n+1}\circ\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle=H^{n-1}\circ(\pi^{n+1}_{n}\times\mathrm{id})\circ(\pi_{n+1}\times\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle=H^{n-1}\circ(\pi_{n}\times\mathrm{id})

\displaystyle=(\pi_{n}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{n-1}\,.

Thus the family
\big\{(\pi_{n+1}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}H^{n}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow T(J^{n}_{M}F)\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}
uniquely corresponds to a
map
H:J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\rightarrow T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.
Following the formal sum representation from (
45
), this map may be represented in local coordinates (plot-wise)

\displaystyle\bigg(s^{k}_{\epsilon}\,,\,X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{\pi^{\infty}_{M}(s^{k}_{\epsilon})}\bigg)\;\longmapsto\;X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{s^{k}_{\epsilon}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}s^{a}_{I+\mu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{s^{k}_{\epsilon}}\bigg)\,,

(79)

by which it easily follows that it splits the sequence (
66
) (cf.
[
Part I
, (78),(79)]
).
∎

Finally, let us also fully characterize the synthetic pushforward (
28
)

\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\phi)\;:\;TM\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}TJ^{\infty}_{M}F

along the infinite jet prolongation
j^{\infty}\phi\,:\,M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
of any field configuration
\phi\in\mathcal{F}(*)
.

Corollary 3.3

(
Synthetic pushfoward of jet prolongated section).

(i)

The synthetic pushforward of the infinite jet prolongation
j^{\infty}\phi:M\rightarrow F
of a field configuration
\phi\in\Gamma_{M}(F)
is equivalently represented by the family of (traditional) pushforwards

\big\{\mathrm{d}j^{n}\phi:TM\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}TJ^{n}_{M}F\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}

of smooth manifolds.

(ii)

Furthermore, it is equivalently given by the composition

H\circ(j^{\infty}\phi,\,\mathrm{id}_{TM})\circ(\pi_{M},\,\mathrm{id}_{TM})\quad:\quad TM\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F\times_{M}TM\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}HJ^{\infty}_{M}F\hookrightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\,,

and hence in particular factors through the horizontal subbundle of
TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
.

(iii)

It follows that this is represented in local coordinates as

X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{p}\;\longmapsto\;X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\phi^{a}}{\partial x^{I+\mu}}(p)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\bigg)\,,

and similarly on arbitrary plots (cf. (
79
)).

Proof.

The first claim follows immediately by limit characterization of
TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
from Lem.
2.34
, and the fact that the synthetic pushforward along maps of
finite-dimensional manifolds recovers the traditional pushfoward between their tangent bundles (
29
). The second claim follows immediately by the definition of the splitting map (
78
) (cf. (
3.1
)), which also yields the local coordinate representation by (
79
).
∎

Forms and vector fields on the infinite jet bundle.

Differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
are defined analogously as maps of thickened smooth sets out of its (synthetic) tangent bundle. The subalgebra of global finite order forms (cf. Rem.
3.5
) is identified precisely with the corresponding notion appearing within smooth sets (
[
Part I
, §4.3]
), and hence the corresponding Cartan calculus and cohomology results follow verbatim. For a more expansive discussion of the standard concepts recalled below, and their relation to the traditional literature, the interested reader may consult
[
Part I
]
.

Definition 3.4

(
Forms on the infinite jet bundle).

The set of differential m-forms on the infinite jet bundle is defined as

\displaystyle\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;:=\;\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.an.}}_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,\mathbbm{R}\big)\,,

(80)

i.e., real-valued, fiber-wise
\mathbbm{R}
-linear antisymmetric maps of thickened smooth sets.
For
p+q=m
, a differential
m
-form
\omega\in\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
on the infinite jet bundle is called:

(i)

p
-
horizontal
if the following restriction is the zero map

\omega\;:\;V^{\times p}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}T^{\times q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbbm{R}\;;

(ii)

q
-
vertical
if the following restriction is the zero map

\omega\;:\;H^{\times q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\times_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}T^{\times p}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbbm{R}\;;

(iii)

a
(p,q)
-form if it is both
p
-horizontal
and

q
-vertical.

Remark 3.5

(
Point-set characterization of forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
).

Recall that smooth functions
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
, defined within pure smooth sets
\mathrm{SmthSet}
, are completely characterized via their point-set description as those of locally finite order, due to the fully faithful embedding
\mathrm{FrMfd}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{SmthSet}
(
[
Part I
, Prop. 3.7, Rem. 3.8]
) and the natural Fréchet (locally pro-) manifold structure on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
. In particular, these include those of globally finite order, which are the only ones appearing in examples from field theory.

(i)

On abstract grounds, there exists a fully faithful functor
27
27

27

This is given by the left Kan extension along
\iota:\mathrm{CartSp}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
from (
7
), composed with sheafication
L:\mathrm{PreSh}(\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp})\rightarrow\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp})
.

\iota_{!}:\mathrm{SmthSet}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, which hence fully faithfully embeds also Fréchet manifolds into thickened smooth manifolds and so produces an – a-priori different – version of the thickened infinite jet bundle
\iota_{!}\big(\lim_{\mathrm{SmthSet}}J^{k}_{M}F\big)
.

The fact that this version of the jet bundle is the same as that from Def.
2.33
, i.e., that passage from the category of smooth Fréchet manifolds to the
Cahiers topos
of
thickened smooth sets

preserves the projective limits that define infinite jet bundles, is proved in
[
GKSS25
]
. This implies that smooth functions on the jet bundle defined in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, and similarly differential forms on it, are again fully characterized as being exhausted by the point-set
locally
finite order functions and differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
.

(ii)

Nevertheless, both for our purposes here and for the sake of exposition, the weaker statement that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F,\,\mathbbm{R})
(injectively) includes the subalgebra of
globally
finite order functions

C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F,\,\mathbbm{R}){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F,\,\mathbbm{R})\,,

is sufficient. But this is obviously true, precisely as in
\mathrm{SmthSet}
, by pulling back finite order functions
J^{n}_{M}F\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
along the canonical projections
\pi^{\infty}_{n}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow J^{n}_{M}F
, now interpreted as maps of thickened smooth sets (cf. Def.
2.33
, Ex.
2.13
). Analogously, the same applies for differential forms defined within thickened smooth sets, but focusing on the globally finite order forms

\Omega^{m}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

(iii)

From now onwards, in order to ease the descriptive adjectives, we will often abusively speak of ‘(smooth)
m
-forms and functions’ on the thickened infinite jet bundle to mean only those of globally finite order, bearing in mind that in reality these form the larger set of
locally
finite order forms.

Henceforth, we shall focus on globally finite order forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
. Any such
m
-form

\omega\,:=(\pi^{\infty}_{n})^{*}\omega_{n}\,\;:\;T^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xlongrightarrow{\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{n}\,}T^{\times m}(J^{n}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\,\,\omega_{n}\,\,}\mathbbm{R}

is represented in local coordinates (of
J^{n}_{M}F
) as

\displaystyle\omega=\sum_{p+q=m}\,\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}^{n}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{a_{1}}_{I_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}u^{a_{q}}_{I_{q}}\,,

(81)

due to the fully faithful embedding from Ex.
2.13
. Already by this local representation, the wedge product structure

\wedge\;\;:\;\;\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\times\;\Omega^{m^{\prime}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{m+m^{\prime}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

becomes immediate, and similarly for the de Rham differential

\mathrm{d}\;:\;\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{m+1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

acting locally as

\mathrm{d}\omega\,=\,\bigg(\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\bigg)\omega\,.

Remark 3.6

(
Forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
via the moduli space).

Note that the proof of
[
Part I
, Lem. 4.15]
holds identically in the thickened smooth setting, exactly by the same formal reasons, thus yielding a canonical DGCA injection

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

into the de Rham forms (
15
) of the infinite jet bundle.

Definition 3.7

(
Vector fields on infinite jet bundle).

The set of (smooth, thickened) vector fields on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
is defined as

\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F):=\Gamma_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}(TJ^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

that is, as sections of its tangent bundle

\textstyle{TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{J^{\infty}_{M}F}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\,}

within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
.

Let us expand on the definition. By the limit property of
TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
from Lem.
2.34
, a vector field
X\in\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
is equivalently given by a
compatible family of maps
\{X:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow TJ^{n}_{M}F\}_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}
, such that the diagrams

\textstyle{TJ^{n}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\pi^{n}_{n-1}}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 16.38895ptX^{n-1}}
\textstyle{TJ^{n-1}_{M}F\mathrlap{\;,}}

\textstyle{TJ^{n}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 16.38895pt\pi^{\infty}_{n}}
\textstyle{J^{n}_{M}F}

commute for all
n\in\mathbbm{N}
.

Note Rem.
3.5
applies verbatim for each member
X^{n}
of such a family. As with differential forms, even if these are exhausted by those of locally finite order, we focus on the physically relevant case of globally finite order vector fields

\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\subset\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

where each of the vector fields in the family is actually of finite order, i.e., a pullback

X^{n}\,\equiv\,X^{n}_{j_{n}}\circ\pi^{\infty}_{n}\;\;:\;\;J^{\infty}_{M}F\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{j_{n}}_{M}F\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}TJ^{n}_{M}F\,.

In this case, by the fully faithful embedding of Ex.
2.13
, such a family

\big\{X^{n}:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow T(J^{n}_{M}F)\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}

may be represented in local coordinates by an infinite (formal) sum

\displaystyle X=X^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}Y^{a}_{I}\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\,,

(82)

for an infinite list of finite order smooth functions
\big\{X^{\mu},\{Y_{I}^{a}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big\}\subset C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
,
with every
X^{n}
corresponding to the case where the sum is terminated at order
|I|=k
(cf.
[
Part I
, (67)]
). It follows directly (e.g.,
[
Part I
, Lem. 4.5]
) that vector fields are in bijection with derivations of functions on
J^{\infty}_{M}F

\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\cong\;\mathrm{Der}\big(C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big)\,.

Similarly, it follows that (cf.
[
Part I
, Rem 4.12]
)

\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\;\cong\;\;\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mbox{-}\mathrm{Mod}}\Big(\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\Big)\,

as modules over
C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, and hence further that

\displaystyle\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;

\displaystyle\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\mbox{-}\mathrm{Mod}}^{\mathrm{antis.}}\Big(\mathcal{X}^{\times m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,\,C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\Big)\,\

(83)

\displaystyle\cong\;\bigwedge^{m}_{C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)}\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

The isomorphisms here are witnessed via the contraction operator

\displaystyle\iota_{X}\,:\,\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\omega\circ X

whose form in local coordinates follows exactly as in finite-dimensional manifolds using (
81
) and (
82
), and which extends as a (graded) derivation to a map

\iota_{X}\;:\;\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{m-1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,.

The Lie derivative operator is then defined as

\mathbb{L}_{X}\,:=\,[\mathrm{d},\iota_{X}]\;:\;\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{m}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

whose form in local coordinates is an infinite-dimensional manifold, and which satisfies the corresponding Cartan calculus identities.

Variational bicomplex.

By the same arguments as in
[
Part I
, §5]
, the bicomplex of globally (or locally) finite order forms may now be seen as arising completely within thickened smooth sets, along with the all-important corresponding results on vertical and horizontal cohomologies.
In more detail, by the splitting of Prop.
3.2
, the module of vector fields splits into vertical and horizontal parts

\mathcal{X}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\cong\;\mathcal{X}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,\oplus\,\mathcal{X}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

which in the local coordinate representation of (
82
) translates to the splitting of any vector field
X=X_{H}+X_{V}
with

\displaystyle X_{H}=X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\bigg)\,,\qquad X_{V}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\big(Y_{I}^{a}-X^{\mu}\cdot u^{a}_{I+\mu}\big)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\,.

(84)

Similarly, by the identification of
m
-forms from (
83
), it follows that (globally finite order) 1-forms split into horizontal and vertical parts
\big((1,0)-
and
(0,1)-
forms respectively, as per Def.
3.4
\big)

\displaystyle\Omega^{1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\cong\;\Omega^{1}_{H}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\oplus\Omega^{1}_{V}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

which in the local coordinate representation from (
81
) translates to the splitting of any
1
-form
\omega=\omega_{H}+\omega_{V}
with

\displaystyle\omega_{H}=(\omega_{H})_{\mu}\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\omega_{V}=\sum_{|I|=0}^{n}(\omega_{V})_{a}^{I}\cdot(\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}-u^{a}_{I+\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=:\sum_{|I|=0}^{n}(\omega_{V})_{a}^{I}\cdot\theta^{a}_{I}\,.

(85)

This further implies that the differential forms
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
carry a bialgebra structure, as they split into the sum of
(p,q)
-forms (Def.
3.4
)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\cong\;\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;:=\;\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbbm{N}}\bigoplus_{p+q=m}\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

where in local coordinates a
(p,q)
-form
\omega\in\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
takes the form

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle=\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}^{n}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\theta^{a_{1}}_{I_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\theta^{a_{q}}_{I_{q}}\,.

(86)

By appropriately projecting into the corresponding subspaces (
[
Part I
, Def 5.3]
), the de Rham differential splits
into a horizontal and vertical differential

\mathrm{d}=\mathrm{d}_{H}+\mathrm{d}_{V}

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}:\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{p+1,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\hskip 42.67912pt\mathrm{d}_{V}:\Omega^{p,q}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{p,q+1}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\,,

which are completely determined by their action in local coordinates as:

\mathrm{d}_{H}(f)=\bigg(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{\mu}}+\sum_{I=0}^{n}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u_{I}^{a}}\bigg)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}=:D_{\mu}(f)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{\mu}\,,\qquad\mathrm{d}_{V}(f)=\sum_{|I|=0}^{n}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\cdot(\mathrm{d}u^{a}_{I}-u^{a}_{I+\mu}\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=:\sum_{|I|=0}^{n}\frac{\partial f}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\cdot\theta^{a}_{I}\,,

for any
f\in C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
, and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=0,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(\mathrm{d}x^{\mu})=0,\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{H}(\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I})=-\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I+\mu}\wedge\mathrm{d}x^{\mu},\hskip 28.45274pt\mathrm{d}_{V}(\mathrm{d}_{V}u^{a}_{I})=0\,.

These satisfy
\mathrm{d}_{H}^{2}=\mathrm{d}_{V}^{2}=\mathrm{d}_{H}\circ\mathrm{d}_{V}+\mathrm{d}_{V}\circ\mathrm{d}_{H}=0
, thus defining the “
variational bicomplex
” (
[
Part I
, Def. 5.4]
)

\displaystyle\big(\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F),\,\mathrm{d}_{H},\,\mathrm{d}_{V}\big)\,.

(87)

It follows that Takens’ Acyclicity Theorem on the cohomology of the variational bicomplex applies (
[
Part I
, Prop. 5.5]
), and its augmentation (extension) by functional/source forms using the interior Euler operator (
[
Part I
, Def. 5.9, Prop. 5.10]
) follow verbatim, along with the corresponding results on the Euler–Lagrange subcomplex (
[
Part I
, Def. 5.11, Prop. 5.18]
). For the sake of brevity, we shall not review these here.

3.2
Transgression and the local bicomplex

In the same vein, all the definitions and related results regarding local Lagrangians and local currents/functionals (
[
Part I
, §3.2]
), the corresponding (shell and) on-shell spaces of fields (
[
Part I
, §5.2]
), local vector fields and conserved currents (
[
Part I
, §6.1, §6.2]
) follow identically in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, provided the prolongation map

j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Gamma_{M}(J^{\infty}F)

is actually a well-defined map of
thickened
smooth sets, such that its synthetic pushforward (
28
) yields precisely the ‘ad-hoc’ pushforward defined in
[
Part I
, Eq. (76) and Ex. 4.7]
.
More generally, the local bicomplex on
\mathcal{F}\times M
and the corresponding presymplectic structure of local field theories
(
[
Part I
, §7]
) follows verbatim, provided the prolongated evaluation map

\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\;:\;\mathcal{F}\times M\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}J^{\infty}_{M}F

is a well-defined map of thickened smooth sets, such that its synthetic pushforward yields precisely the one from

[
Part I
, Def. 7.4]
.

The fact that the infinite jet prolongation exists as a map of thickened smooth sets follows by internal considerations (cf. Def.
4.31
of Appendix
section

4.3
), i.e., using the viewpoint of jets as sections over infinitesimal neighborhoods from (
2.4
). However, for the sake of making this abstract statement more concrete, we now expand on the definition of infinite jet prolongation in the current setting in a different manner. This boils down to defining it locally over trivializing charts and by a further careful application of the universal property of limits.

To that end, recall (Def.
2.21
) that
any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot
\phi^{k,\epsilon}
of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
is given dually by a ‘pullback’ map of algebras
(\phi^{k,\epsilon})^{*}:C^{\infty}(F)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M)
. To define such a map, it is sufficient to declare its (compatible) action on (a cover of) adapted coordinate functions
\{x^{\mu},u^{a}\}

\displaystyle\big(\phi_{\epsilon}^{k}\big)^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\big)

\displaystyle x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto x^{\mu}

\displaystyle u^{a}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\mathrlap{\,,}

where
\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M)\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
is a smooth (locally and compatibly defined) function on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M
, polynomially extended in
\epsilon\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
.
This holds by the (topological/petit) sheaf properties of the given function algebras.

From this point of view, it is easy to define the (first) jet prolongation map, acting on (plots of) sections as

\displaystyle j^{1}\;:\quad\mathcal{F}\;\;

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{1}F)

\displaystyle\phi^{k,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\longmapsto j^{1}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\mathrlap{\,,}

where
j^{1}\phi^{k,\epsilon}
is the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{1}F)
defined dually by its action on any (cover of) coordinate charts

\displaystyle\big(j^{1}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\big)^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(J^{1}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\big)

\displaystyle x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto x^{\mu}

\displaystyle u^{a}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)

\displaystyle u^{a}_{\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\partial_{\mu}\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\mathrlap{\,,}

where
\partial_{\mu}
denotes the derivations with respect to
x^{\mu}
, i.e. the partial derivatives along
M
on the manifold
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M
, extended trivially along the nilpotent algebra elements of
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
. Since the locally defined target functions are compatible, this glues to a morphism of global functions. This assignment on plots is obviously functorial under maps of probes, and hence defines a map of thickened smooth sets. Completely analogously, the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
-jet prolongation defines a map of thickened smooth sets

\displaystyle j^{n}\;:\quad\mathcal{F}\;\;

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{n}F)

(88)

\displaystyle\phi^{k,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\longmapsto j^{n}\phi^{k,\epsilon}

where
j^{n}\phi^{k,\epsilon}
is defined dually (and locally) by

\displaystyle\big(j^{n}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(J^{n}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\big)

(89)

\displaystyle x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\big\{u^{a}_{I}\big\}_{|I|\leq n}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\big\}_{|I|\leq n}.

The compatibility of finite-order jet prolongations implies the existence of the infinite jet prolongation valued in the thickened smooth set of sections of the infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F
, which we shall henceforth denote as

\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\,:=\,\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)\quad\in\quad\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Lemma 3.8

(
Thickened infinite jet prolongation).

The infinite jet prolongation exists as a map of thickened smooth sets of sections

j^{\infty}\;:\;\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\,.

Proof.

By its defining limit property from Def.
2.33
, any map
\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
of thickened smooth sets is equivalently given by a compatible family of maps of thickened smooth manifolds

\big\{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,n}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{n}_{M}F\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}\,.

It follows that a plot of sections in
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)
, i.e., a map
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
that satisfies the section condition

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{M}}
\textstyle{M\,,}

is equivalently a compatible family of maps of thickened smooth manifolds
