\big\{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,n}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{n}_{M}F\big\}
each of which satisfies the corresponding section condition over
M
.

Thus, given any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot
\phi^{k,\epsilon}
of
\mathcal{F}
the compatible family of sections

\big\{j^{n}\phi^{k,\epsilon}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{n}_{M}F\big\}_{n\in\mathbbm{N}}\,,

obtained by applying each finite jet prolongation (
88
), corresponds precisely to a plot of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)

j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\;:\;\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F

which is functorial under pullbacks of probes, hence defining the desired map of thickened smooth sets

j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\infty}
.
∎

Notice, by (
89
), the infinite jet prolongation of an
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot may be presented dually via its action on the infinite list of local coordinates as

\displaystyle(j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon})^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\big)

(90)

\displaystyle x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\big\{u^{a}_{I}\big\}_{0\leq|I|}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\big\}_{0\leq|I|}\,.

Example 3.9

(
Differential operators and the Euler–Lagrange equation).

With the infinite jet prolongation at hand, we may plot-wise precompose any bundle map
P:J^{\infty}F\rightarrow G
over
M
to identify differential operators as maps of thickened smooth sets of sections (
[
Part I
, Lem. 3.15]
)

\mathcal{P}\,:=\,P\circ j^{\infty}\,.

Of particular importance to field theory is the Euler–Lagrange operator corresponding to the Euler–Lagrange “source” form
EL:=\delta_{V}L\in\Omega^{d,1}_{s}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
(see
[
Part I
, pp. 50-51]
), i.e., equivalently the corresponding bundle map
EL:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
over M, which induces the Euler–Lagrange differential operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\,:=\,EL\circ j^{\infty}\;\;:\quad\mathcal{F}\;\;

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}

(91)

\displaystyle\phi^{k,\epsilon}

\displaystyle\longmapsto EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\,,

defining a section of the variational cotangent bundle (Def.
2.31
). In local coordinates where
L=\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\{u^{a}_{I}\}_{0\leq|I|}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{d}
it follows that
EL=\delta_{V}L=\sum(-1)^{|I|}D_{I}\Big(\!\frac{\partial\bar{L}}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\!\Big)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{F}u^{a}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{n}
, and hence by Eq. (
90
) the result of applying the Euler–Lagrange operator on a
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot of fields may be represented (dually) as (cf.
[
Part I
, (123)]
)

\displaystyle\big(EL\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\big)^{*}\;\;:\;\;C^{\infty}\big(\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\big)

(92)

\displaystyle x^{\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto x^{\mu}

\displaystyle u^{a}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi^{a}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)

\displaystyle\hat{u}^{a}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|I|}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\Bigg(\frac{\delta\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\{\partial_{J}\phi^{b}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\Bigg)\,,

where
\{\hat{u}^{a}\}
are the coordinates along the fibers of
\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
, adapted to the canonical coordinate associated to
\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{n}
for the chosen base coordinates
\{x^{\mu}\}
. Abusing notation (even more), we may denote all of this simply as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k,\epsilon})\,=\,\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{|I|}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{I}}\Bigg(\frac{\delta\bar{L}\big(x^{\mu},\{\partial_{J}\phi^{b}(x^{\mu},c^{k},\epsilon)\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{a})}\Bigg)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{F}u^{a}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}x^{n}\,,

(93)

so that one may think of the (thickened, smooth) Euler–Lagrange operator acting on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of fields as usual along spacetime directions, and carrying along the parametrizing coordinates of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
.

The vanishing of this map of thickened smooth sets yields the Euler–Lagrange “on-shell” equations

\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}=0

now incarnated within thickened smooth sets. We return to the proper description of this thickened zero-locus in Thm.
3.17
as a pullback/intersection of thickened smooth sets, where we furthermore identify it as the expected critical locus of the corresponding local action functional.

We now turn to the synthetic pushforward (
28
) of the infinite jet prolongation map. To that end, let us note the immediate analog result for the synthetic tangent bundle of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)
.

Lemma 3.10

(
Synthetic tangent bundle of jet bundle sections).

The synthetic tangent bundle
T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\>=\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\,\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\big]
of the space of jet bundle sections is naturally identified with the space of jet bundle sections of the vertical fiber bundle
VF\rightarrow F\rightarrow M
, i.e.,

T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\,\cong\,\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}VF)\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Proof.

Working precisely as in the proof of Prop.
2.28
(cf. also Rem.
2.30
) yields the tangent bundle in question equivalently as the pullback

\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[M,\,TJ^{\infty}_{M}F]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M})_{*}}
\textstyle{0_{M}\cong*\;\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[M,\,TM]\,.}

By definition then, analogously to the case of
T\mathcal{F}
, the plots of
T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}
correspond to maps
X_{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\rightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
making the diagram

\textstyle{TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}\pi^{\infty}_{M}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\mathrm{pr}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{X_{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}}}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{M}}
\textstyle{TM\,}

commute, and hence factoring through the vertical subbundle
VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\hookrightarrow TJ^{\infty}_{M}F
by (
3.1
). Under the canonical isomorphism
VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong J^{\infty}_{M}VF
from Lem.
3.1
, these maps correspond exactly to the commuting diagrams of the form

\textstyle{VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong J^{\infty}_{M}VF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X_{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{M}}
\textstyle{M\mathrlap{\,,}}

i.e., precisely the defining plots of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}VF)
.
∎

Proposition 3.11

(
Synthetic pushforward of jet prolongation).

The synthetic pushforward

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}j^{\infty}_{F}\,:\,T\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}

of the jet prolongation map
j^{\infty}_{F}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\infty}

is given, under the identifications of Prop.
2.28
and Lem.
2.34
, precisely by the corresponding prolongation map

\displaystyle j^{\infty}_{VF}\,:\,\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VF)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}VF)\,.

That is, the following diagram commutes

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 22.38962pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.88022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.25002pt\raise-9.77776pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-11.61105pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.98721pt\raise 6.26584pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}j^{\infty}_{F}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.53008pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.96732pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 99.53008pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 113.64642pt\raise-9.77776pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 113.64642pt\raise-11.61105pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-9.61108pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-22.38962pt\raise-19.55551pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VF)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.49414pt\raise-13.28967pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90474pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}_{VF}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 81.54501pt\raise-19.55551pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.96732pt\raise-19.55551pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 81.54501pt\raise-19.55551pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}VF)\,.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

Proof.

This follows by chasing through the given identifications. More explicitly, the synthetic pushforward (
28
)
\mathrm{d}j^{\infty}_{F}
acts on
any plot
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}
of the tangent bundle, i.e., equivalently on any section

\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{M}}
\textstyle{M\,,}

by postcomposition, thus yielding a prolongated section

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\infty}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}_{F}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{(}\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{)}\;\;\;}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{M}}
\textstyle{M\,,}

which is by definition a plot
j^{\infty}_{F}(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}):\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow T\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)
, covering the plot
j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\infty}
. But by the identification of Lem.
4.24
, along with the defining expression of
j^{\infty}
in local coordinates (
90
), this is identified precisely with the section

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{M}VF\cong VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\infty}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}_{VF}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{(}\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{)}\;\;\;\;}
\scriptstyle{p_{M}}
\textstyle{M\,,}

where now
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}
is viewed as a plot of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VF)
under the identification of Prop.
2.28
, and hence acted upon with the corresponding jet prolongation.
∎

Let us now turn to the matter of the prolongated evaluation map within thickened smooth sets. This is defined by the composition

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\;:\;\mathcal{F}\times M\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt(j^{\infty},\,\mathrm{id}_{M})\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\times M\xlongrightarrow{\;\;\mathrm{ev}\;\;}J^{\infty}_{M}F\,.

(94)

where the (canonical) evaluation map explicitly acts on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots as

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}\;:\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)\times M

\displaystyle\;\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\;J^{\infty}_{M}F

(95)

\displaystyle\big(\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty},\,p^{k,\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\;{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\;\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}},\,p^{k,\epsilon}\big)\,,

where the pair of plots are maps
\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
and
p^{k,\epsilon}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow M
, so that the composition above yields a plot
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
of the jet bundle. At the level of
*
-plots, this of course encompasses the usual set-theoretic evaluation map

(\psi^{\infty},p){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\psi^{\infty}(p)\quad\in\quad J^{\infty}_{M}F(*)\,.

By expanding this definition carefully in local coordinates, it is easy to see that the plots in the image of the prolongated evaluation map (
94
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\times M

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}J^{\infty}_{M}F

\displaystyle\big(\phi^{k,\epsilon},\,p^{k,\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}},\,p^{k,\epsilon}\big)

may be represented dually (and locally), using (
90
), as

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)

(96)

\displaystyle x^{\mu}

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}p^{\mu}(c^{k},\epsilon)

\displaystyle\big\{u^{a}_{I}\big\}_{0\leq|I|}

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\big\{\partial_{I}\phi^{a}\big(p^{\mu}(c^{k},\epsilon),\,c^{k},\epsilon\big)\big\}_{0\leq|I|}\,.

Having defined prolongated evaluation map within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, we may apply the synthetic tangent functor

T=[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\,-]
to obtain the synthetic pushforward (
28
)

\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\;:\;T(\mathcal{F}\times M)\cong T\mathcal{F}\times TM\xrightarrow{\hskip 16.38895pt}TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\,.

In order to derive a more concrete description plot-wise for this pushforward, we first describe the pushforward along the usual evaluation maps (
95
).

Proposition 3.12

(
Pushforward of evaluation map).

(i)
For the evaluation map
\mathrm{ev}:\mathcal{F}\times M\rightarrow F
of a finite-dimensional fiber bundle
F\rightarrow M
(cf. (
95
)), the corresponding synthetic pushforward is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}\;:\quad T\mathcal{F}\times

\displaystyle TM\quad\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\quad TF

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}},

\displaystyle\,X_{p^{k,\epsilon}}\big){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{d}_{M}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}},X_{p^{k,\epsilon}}\big)\;\;+\;\;\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}}\times p^{k,\epsilon}\big)\,.

(ii)
Similarly, for the evaluation map
\mathrm{ev}:\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
of the infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow M
(
95
), the corresponding synthetic pushforward is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}\;:\quad T\mathcal{F}^{\infty}\times

\displaystyle TM\quad\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\quad TJ^{\infty}_{M}F

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}},

\displaystyle\,X_{p^{k,\epsilon}}\big){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{d}_{M}\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}},X_{p^{k,\epsilon}}\big)\;\;+\;\;\mathcal{Z}_{\psi^{k,\epsilon,\,\infty}}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}}\times p^{k,\epsilon}\big).

Proof.

For the sake of transparency, we show this at the level of
*
-plots, with that of general
(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
-plots following similarly by (trivially) carrying along the dependence along the probe-legs. By definition, a tangent vector on
\mathcal{F}\times M
is a pair of maps
(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi},X_{p})\in T\mathcal{F}\times TM
where

\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}:\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow F

covers
\mathrm{pr}_{M}:\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times M\rightarrow M
, and

X_{p}:\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\,.

Now, by definition of the synthetic pushfoward (
28
) acts via postcomposition and using (
95
), we get

\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi},X_{p}\big):=\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\times X_{p}\big)\;\;:\;\;\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow F\,.

Each of the maps in this composition is given dually by

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\times X_{p}\big)^{*}\;\;:\;\;\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\big)\otimes C^{\infty}(M)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\big)

\displaystyle f+\epsilon\cdot g

\displaystyle\longmapsto f(p)+\epsilon\cdot\big(X_{p}(f)+g(p)\big)\,,

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(F)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\big)\otimes C^{\infty}(M)

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\longmapsto h\circ\phi+\epsilon\cdot\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}(h)

where
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}(h)
is the function on
M
given by
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}(h)(p):=\mathcal{Z}_{\phi(p)}(h)
, i.e., by acting point-wise with the corresponding (vertical) tangent vector (equiv. derivation)
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi(p)}\in V_{p}F
. It follows that
\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi},\,X_{p}):\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\rightarrow F
is given dually as

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(F)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\big)

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;(h\circ\phi)(p)+\epsilon\cdot\big(X_{p}(h\circ\phi)+\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}(h)(p)\big)\;=\;h\big(\phi(p))+\epsilon\cdot\big(\mathrm{d}\phi_{p}X_{p}+\mathcal{Z}_{\phi(p)}(h)\big)\,,

which is precisely the derivation corresponding to the tangent vector

\mathrm{d}\phi_{p}(X_{p})\;\;+\;\;\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}(p)\;\;\in\;\;T_{\phi(p)}F\,,

confirming the first claim.

Notice that by the first claim the formula holds for each finite-order jet evaluation map
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{n}F)\times M\rightarrow J^{n}_{M}F
, and hence the second claim follows by the limit property of
J^{\infty}_{M}F
and its tangent bundle’s
TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong\lim TJ^{n}_{M}F
from Lem.
2.34
,
along with the identification
VJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong J^{\infty}_{M}VF
from Lem.
3.1
.
∎

Corollary 3.13

(
Pushforward of prolongated evaluation).

(i)

The synthetic pushforward of the prolongated evaluation map
\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\times M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}F
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}\;:\quad T\mathcal{F}\times

\displaystyle TM\quad\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\quad TJ^{\infty}_{M}F\cong HJ^{\infty}_{M}F\oplus J^{\infty}_{M}VF

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}},

\displaystyle\,X_{p^{k,\epsilon}}\big){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{d}_{M}\big(j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\big)\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}},X_{p^{k,\epsilon}}\big)\;\;+\;\;j^{\infty}\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\circ\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}}\times p^{k,\epsilon}\big)\,,

with each of the summands factoring through the horizontal and vertical subbundles, respectively.

(ii)

In (compatible) local coordinates where
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}=\mathcal{Z}^{a}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\delta\phi^{a}}:=\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{a}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}}\in T_{\phi}\mathcal{F}\cong\Gamma_{M}(\phi^{*}VF)

and
X_{p}=X^{\mu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}|_{p}

\in T_{p}M
, the pushforward is represented by

(
cf. Cor.
3.3
(iii)
)

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt\bigg(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{a}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}},\,X^{\mu}\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{p}\bigg)\quad\longmapsto\quad X^{\mu}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}+\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\phi^{a}}{\partial x^{I+\mu}}(p)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\bigg)+\;\sum_{|I|=0}^{\infty}\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi}^{a}}{\partial x^{I}}(p)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{a}_{I}}\Big|_{j^{\infty}_{p}\phi}\,,

(97)

and similarly for arbitrary plots.

Proof.

By the functoriality of the synthetic pushforward (
28
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}^{\infty}=\mathrm{d}\big(\mathrm{ev}\circ(j^{\infty}\times\mathrm{id}_{M})\big)=\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}\circ\mathrm{d}(j^{\infty}\times\mathrm{id}_{M})=\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}\circ\big(\mathrm{d}j^{\infty}+\mathrm{id}_{TM}\big)\,,

whereby the result follows by Prop.
3.3
and Prop.
3.12
, with the first summand taking values in the horizontal subspace by Lem.
3.3
.
∎

Remark 3.14

(
Pushforwards via derivatives of line plots).

We note that the above sequence of results may be equivalently proved by representing any plot of tangent vectors via a line plot
\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}
(Lem.
2.29
), and taking the corresponding line-parameter derivative of the jet prolongated line plot
j^{\infty}_{F}\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}
, which is precisely the intuition used to motivate the (therein ad-hoc) form of the pushforward in

[
Part I
, Ex. 4.7, Def. 7.4]
. Here, we have recovered it naturally and rigorously as an example of the synthetic pushforward construction; hence being already in its extended thickened version.

The bicomplex of local forms and its Cartan calculus.
The traditional local bicomplex (of globally f.o. forms) on
\mathcal{F}\times M

(
[
Part I
, Def. 7.5]
) now appears naturally within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, as the pullback of the variational bi-complex on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
along the prolongated evaluation map, by employing its
synthetic
pushforward (Cor.
3.13
)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,:

\displaystyle=\,(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{glb}}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\;\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\;\Omega^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\,,

(98)

where

\displaystyle(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\,:\,\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{\bullet}(\mathcal{F}\times M)

\displaystyle\omega

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\omega\circ\mathrm{d}\mathrm{ev}\,.

By the local coordinate expressions of tangent vectors on
\mathcal{F}\times M
and the pushfoward from (
97
), along with that of differential forms on
J^{\infty}_{M}F
from (
86
), it follows that any local such form be represented locally as

(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega=\sum_{I_{1},\cdots,I_{p}=0}\omega_{\mu_{1}\cdots\mu_{p}a_{1}\cdots a_{q}}^{I_{1}\dots I_{q}}\big(x,\{\partial_{J}\phi^{b}\}_{|J|\leq k}\big)\cdot\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\mathrm{d}_{M}x^{\mu_{p}}\wedge\delta(\partial_{I_{1}}\phi^{a_{1}})\wedge\cdots\wedge\delta(\partial_{I_{q}}\phi^{a_{q}})\,.

The vertical and horizontal differentials are given by pulling back those of the variational bicomplex

\delta\big((\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega\big)\,:=\,(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\mathrm{d}_{V}\omega\quad\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\quad\mathrm{d}_{M}\big((\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\omega\big)\,:=\,(\mathrm{ev}^{\infty})^{*}\mathrm{d}_{M}\omega

and they act as traditionally expected in terms of local coordinate expressions.
We shall not repeat this discussion here, as it follows verbatim as in
[
Part I
, pp. 89-91]
. The same applies for the discussion regarding the corresponding local Cartan calculus (
[
Part I
, p. 92]
) with insertions of local vector fields
\mathcal{Z}=Z\circ j^{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}(\mathcal{F}\times M)

\iota_{\mathcal{Z}}\,:\,\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{\bullet-1,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)

and Lie derivative

\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{Z}}:=[\iota_{\mathcal{Z}},\delta]\;\;:\;\;\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)

and hence for the result of
[
Part I
, Prop. 7.7, cf. Rem. 7.8]
as well.

Remark 3.15

(
Local forms on
\mathcal{F}\times M
via the moduli space).

Just as in the case of forms on the jet bundle (Rem.
3.6
), the proof of
[
Part I
, Lem. 7.9]
holds for the same formal reasons, hence yielding a DGCA injection of local forms

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)

into the de Rham forms (
15
) on
\mathcal{F}\times M
.

3.3
On-shell fields as a thickened smooth critical set

Smooth Lagrangian bundle maps
L:J^{\infty}_{M}F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}(T^{*}M)
, equivalently differential forms
L\in\Omega^{0,d}(J^{\infty}_{M}F
), may now be considered to be defined within thickened smooth sets. It follows that the (plot-wise) precomposition with the thickened jet prolongation
j^{\infty}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\infty}
(Lem.
3.8
) yields the local Lagrangian densities of physical theories as per
[
Part I
, Def. 3.12]

\mathcal{L}\,:=\,L\circ j^{\infty}\;\;:\;\;\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)\,,

or equivalently as a horizontal local top-form

\mathcal{L}\in\Omega^{0,d}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M).

Similarly, one obtains local currents on field space
[
Part I
, Def. 3.17]
by precomposing
(p,0)
-forms
P\in\Omega^{0,p}(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
with the thickened jet prolongation

\mathcal{P}\,:=\,P\circ j^{\infty}\;\;:\;\;\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)\,,

or equivalently
\mathcal{P}\in\Omega^{0,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
.

For any compact oriented submanifold
\Sigma^{p}\hookrightarrow M
, the notion of integration defines a map of thickened smooth sets

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\,:\;\;\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbbm{R}

(99)

extending that of plain smooth sets from
[
Part I
, (57)]
. This is defined by plot-wise integration, simply by carrying along the (finite) polynomial dependence on the nilpotent coordinates.
Explicitly, for any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-parametrized p-form on
M

\omega^{k,\epsilon}\,=\,\sum_{|I|=1}^{l}\omega^{k}_{I}\cdot\epsilon^{I}\quad\in\quad\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})

where for each
\omega^{k}_{I}\in\Omega^{p}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, the value
of the function
\int_{M}\omega^{k,\epsilon}\,\in\,y(\mathbbm{R})(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
is given by

\int_{M}\omega^{k,\epsilon}\;\;:=\;\;\sum_{|I|=1}^{l}\;\bigg(\int_{M}\omega^{k}_{I}\!\bigg)\cdot\epsilon^{I}\quad

where
\big(\int_{M}\omega^{k}_{I}\big)(x):=\int_{M}\iota_{x}^{*}(\omega^{k}_{I})
, with
\iota_{x}
being the inclusion
M\xrightarrow{\sim}\{x\}\times M\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times M
. With integration within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
at hand, the definition of charges of local currents along submanifolds follows as in
[
Part I
, Def. 3.19]

\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma^{p}}\,:=\,\int_{\Sigma^{p}}\circ\;\;\mathcal{P}\quad:\quad\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbbm{R}\,,

and similarly for the algebra of
local functionals

C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathcal{F})
generated by these
[
Part I
, Def. 3.20]
.

Assuming the spacetime manifold
M
is compact without boundary
28
28

28

Otherwise one works over arbitrary compact, oriented
d
-dimensional submanifolds
\Sigma^{d}\hookrightarrow M
, with a slight modification of the ensuing discussion as per
[
Part I
, §5.3]
.

, then the charge of the local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
of any theory is precisely its
local action functional

S\,:=\,\int_{M}\circ\;\;\mathcal{L}\,.

This allows to define the notion of criticality for arbitrary thickened (smooth)
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of fields.

Definition 3.16

(
Critical plots of thickened smooth map).

Let
S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
be a thickened smooth map. The
critical
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots
of
S
is the subset of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots

\displaystyle\mathrm{Crit}(S)\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big):=\Big\{\phi^{k,\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\;\big|\;\partial_{t}S\big(\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}\big)|_{t=0}=0,\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\,\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}\big)\Big\},

(100)

where
\mathcal{F}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}\big)=\big\{\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}\in\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}\big)\;\big|\;\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t=0}=\phi^{k,\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\big\}
.

As with bare smooth sets, this yields an assignment of sets of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-critical plots

\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\longmapsto\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,,

for each
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
, which for a generic
S
might not be functorial. However, for local action functionals, this is indeed the case, generalizing
[
Part I
, Prop. 5.31]
to yield a thickened critical smooth set.

Theorem 3.17

(
Functoriality of the thickened critical set).

Let
M
be a compact manifold without boundary,
F\rightarrow M
a fiber bundle,
\mathcal{F}
its smooth set of sections and

S:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
a local (action) functional given by

\displaystyle S=\int_{M}\circ\;\;\mathcal{L}

for some local Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)
.
Then:

(i)

The assignment

\displaystyle\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\longmapsto\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

defines a thickened smooth set.

(ii)

The thickened critical smooth set is canonically identified with the on-shell space of fields
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
, defined as the intersection/pullback diagram of thickened smooth sets

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}(S)\cong\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}\,,}

where
T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}
is the variational cotangent bundle (Def.
2.31
) and
\mathcal{EL}
is the (thickened, smooth) Euler–Lagrange differential operator (
91
) of
\mathcal{L}
, and
0_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}
is the zero-section of thickened smooth sets, defined plot-wise by
\phi^{k,\epsilon}\mapstochar\rightarrow 0_{F}\circ\phi^{k,\epsilon}
where
0_{F}:F\rightarrow\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\otimes V^{*}F
is the zero section of the vector bundle (of manifolds) over F.

Proof.

Having appropriately imported all relevant notions related to the infinite jet bundle, its variational bicomplex, and the jet prolongation within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
(p. 19-25), the proof from
[
Part I
, Prop. 5.31]
in the purely smooth setting applies verbatim for
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots up to the point of yielding

\displaystyle\partial_{t}S\big(\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}\big)|_{t=0}\,=\,\int_{M}\circ\,\,\Big\langle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(\phi^{k,\epsilon}\big)\,,\,\partial_{t}\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}|_{t=0}\Big\rangle\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,y(\mathbbm{R})(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,,

(101)

now taking values in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
, for any 1-parameter variation
\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}
of a fixed
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot of fields
\phi^{k,\epsilon}\equiv\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t=0}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
. Although we shall not repeat the former discussion here, the final step that follows requires some further technical justification in the thickened setting.

The right-hand side above employs the composition of maps of thickened smooth sets

\int_{M}\circ\,\,\langle-,-\rangle\quad:\quad T\mathcal{F}\times T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Omega^{d}_{\mathrm{vert}}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

where the former is the canonical pairing from (
41
) and the latter is the integration map from (
99
). More explicitly, this means that for any probe
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
, this pairing is the one obtained by extending plot-wise the
non-degenerate
pairing of
*
-plots (by the fundamental Lemma of variational calculus)

\Gamma_{M}(VF)\times\Gamma_{M}\big(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\big)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbbm{R}

using the
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\times\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
-module structure of each of

T\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(VF)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\Gamma_{M}(VF)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})

and
29
29

29

Where
\widehat{\otimes}
denotes the completed projective tensor product.

T\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}\big(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\Gamma_{M}\big(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\big)\,\widehat{\otimes}\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})

via the algebra multiplication on
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
.

In particular, since the original pairing of
*
-plots is non-degenerate as a map of
\mathbbm{R}
-modules, it follows that the pairing defined at each probe stage
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
is
non-degenerate
as a map of
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
-modules.
That is,

\int_{M}\circ\,\,\big\langle\mathcal{B}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\,,\,\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\big\rangle\,=\,0\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\,,

for all
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\in T_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
if and only if

\mathcal{B}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\,=\,0\;\;\in\;\;T^{*}_{\mathrm{var},{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,.

Hence, since every plot of tangent vectors to fields is covered by a path of plots of fields (Lem.
2.29
), it follows by (
101
) that an
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot is critical for the local action functional (Def.
3.16
) if and only if it satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equations:

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\phi^{k,\epsilon}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\quad\iff\quad\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}(\phi^{k,\epsilon})=0_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\;\;\in\;\;

\displaystyle T^{*}_{\mathrm{var},{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

(102)

\displaystyle\cong\;\Gamma_{M}\big(V^{*}F\otimes\wedge^{d}T^{*}M\big)\hat{\otimes}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{D})\,,

where
0_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}
is the canonical section given by composing
\phi^{k,\epsilon}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times M\rightarrow F
with the zero-section
0_{F}:F\rightarrow VF
.
By the very construction of the Euler–Lagrange map within thickened smooth sets (Eqs. (
91
)-(
93
)), the latter condition is
functorial
under pullbacks along maps
of plots
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
, and so
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(\phi^{k,\epsilon}\big)=0_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\implies\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\big(f^{*}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\big)=0_{f^{*}\phi^{k,\epsilon}}
.
Hence if

\phi^{k,\epsilon}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
, then
f^{*}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\in\mathrm{Crit}(S)(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})

for all
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
, and so the assignment of critical plots defines a presheaf on
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
.

Finally, since limits are in sheaf categories are computed probe-wise, the probe-wise vanishing condition of the Euler–Lagrange operator is nothing but the defining condition of the corresponding pullback diagram, which also guarantees the sheaf condition and completes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.18

(
de Rham differential of action functional).

It is in our current setting of synthetic differential geometry that Lawvere
[
La80
]
envisaged that the moduli space of one forms
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}
(Def.
4.4
, cf.
section

4.4
) might allow for “a drastic simplification of the usual
differential form calculus”. Unfortunately, this does
not
seem to be the case for the purposes of variational calculus and the correct thickened smooth structure on the critical locus. Namely, one may instead formally compute the composition

\mathrm{d}_{\mathrm{d}R}\circ S\;:\;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}\,,

whose (naive) vanishing condition, however, fails to even produce the correct smooth structure on the critical locus (see
[
Part I
, Rem. 5.41]
).
By abstract reasons
section

4.4
, or by the explicit calculation above, this does encode a
1
-form in the sense of a fiber-wise linear map

\delta S\,:\,T\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,.

Still, however, its further characterization given by a pairing as in (
101
) remains crucial for identifying the correct thickened smooth set structure on its critical locus (cf. Cor.
3.20
)!

Remark 3.19

(
Criticality on general spacetimes).

The result holds similarly on non-compact spacetimes, along the lines of
[
Part I
, Prop. 5.39]
, by first appropriately criticality locally and jointly for all compact
d
-dimensional submanifolds
[
Part I
, Def. 5.37, Def. 5.38]
. The proof then follows verbatim using the petit sheaf nature of
\mathcal{F}
over
M
, given the extra technical justifications of Thm.
3.17
, so we shall not repeat this here. In particular, the critical locus of a local Lagrangian on any non-compact spacetime is still identified as the Euler–Lagrange zero locus, i.e, as the pullback diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\cong\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}\,}

of thickened smooth sets. The discussion further extends to spacetimes with boundary (Ex.
2.14
) as per
[
Part I
, Rem. 5.40]
, now interpreted within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
.

With the above results at hand, we are now able to rigorously recover the pushforward of the Euler–Lagrange operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}

[
Part I
, Def. 7.12]
and the corresponding on-shell tangent bundle to field space
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}

[
Part I
, Def. 7.13]
as a direct application of the synthetic tangent functor (Def. (
2.24
)). Crucially, both of these were defined in a rather ad-hoc manner in the purely smooth setting of
[
Part I
]
, and more so in all previous rigorous approaches to classical field theory we are aware of (if at all defined therein). The result below thus further justifies the intuition that infinitesimals, in the vein of synthetic differential geometry, are indeed necessary for the complete and rigorous description of field theory.

Corollary 3.20

(
Synthetic tangent bundle of on-shell fields is the space of Jacobi fields).

The synthetic tangent bundle
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}:=[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\,,\,\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}]
to the subspace of on-shell fields is canonically identified as the pullback

\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}_{*}}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{0_{\mathcal{F}}}_{*}}
\textstyle{T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\,,}

in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
. Explicitly, this is the thickened smooth set with
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots

T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)=\big\{\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\in T\mathcal{F}\;|\;\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\big)=0_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\big\}\,,

where in local coordinates the synthetic pushforward of the Euler–Lagrange operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}\equiv T(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}\;\;:\;\;T\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\sum_{|I|=0}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi^{k,\epsilon})}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{k,\epsilon,\,b})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}\cdot\mathrm{d}u^{a}\,.

In theoretical physics terminology, the synthetic tangent bundle of the on-shell field space recovers precisely the (thickened, smooth) space of
“Jacobi fields”
.

Proof.

The synthetic tangent bundle of the on-shell field space
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\cong Crit(\mathcal{L})
is obtained by applying the tangent functor
T=[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),-]
to the pullback diagram of Thm.
3.17
and Rem.
3.19
. Using once again that this functor has a left adjoint (
30
), it commutes with all limits and, in particular, pullbacks. Hence it follows that the on-shell synthetic tangent bundle
T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}
is canonically isomorphic to the pullback diagram

\textstyle{T\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{L})\cong T\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}_{*}}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{0_{\mathcal{F}}}_{*}}
\textstyle{T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\,.}

It remains to demonstrate the explicit local form of the synthetic pushforward of the Euler–Lagrange operator
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}\equiv T(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L})
. Recall, this is defined simply by internal post-composition (i.e., plot-wise)

T\mathcal{F}\,:=\,\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\mathcal{F}\big]\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}\circ(-)\hskip 8.19447pt}\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F}\big]\,=:\,T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\,,

and since the projection from path spaces of fields to fields is nothing but the restriction along
\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{1}_{t}
(Eq. (
40
)),
the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathbold{P}(\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{t}|_{t=0}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathbold{P}(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial_{t}|_{t=0}}
\textstyle{T\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{EL}_{*}}
\textstyle{T(T^{*}_{\mathrm{var}}\mathcal{F})\,.}

Thus it follows, since any plot of tangent vectors
\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}
is covered by a path of plots of fields
\phi^{k,\epsilon}_{t}
(Lem.
2.29
), that the pushforward map
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{*}
may be computed by first lifting to a path of plots, and then applying
\partial_{t}|_{t=0}\circ\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}
.
But this is precisely the calculation from
[
Part I
, p. 96]
, extended trivially to carry along the
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
dependence via (
92
)-(
93
), so that by the traditional chain rule (point-wise w.r.t M)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi_{t}^{k,\epsilon})\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}\bigg(\frac{\partial EL_{a}}{\partial u^{b}_{I}}\circ j^{\infty}\phi^{k,\epsilon}\bigg)\cdot\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\frac{\partial\phi^{k,\epsilon,\,b}_{t}}{\partial x^{I}}\Big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|=0}\frac{\delta\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi^{k,\epsilon})}{\delta(\partial_{I}\phi^{k,\epsilon,\,b})}\cdot\frac{\partial\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}^{b}}{\partial x^{I}}

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a})_{*}\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}\big)\;.

∎

Remark 3.21

(
Variations via infinitesimals rigorously).

In local coordinates and by the abuse of notation from Eq. (
93
), it is easily seen that the derivative at
t=0
may be formally computed as the derivative of “
\mathcal{E}\mathcal{L}_{a}(\phi^{k,\epsilon}+\epsilon^{1}\cdot\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}})
” along a nilpotent infinitesimal coordinate
\epsilon^{1}
, i.e.,

\epsilon^{1}\;\;\in\;\;\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1))\cong\mathbbm{R}[\epsilon^{1}]/(\epsilon^{1})^{2},

by Taylor expanding as prescribed above. This is not surprising from our rigorous formalism, since under this abuse of notation
\phi^{k,\epsilon}+\epsilon^{1}\cdot\mathcal{Z}_{\phi^{k,\epsilon}}
stands exactly for an element in
T\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\,\mathcal{F}\big](\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,\cong\,\mathcal{F}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\times\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\big)
. The same holds for the derivative of the action functional from Thm.
3.17
. Thus, these results rigorously justify the formal (and ad-hoc) appearance of the nilpotent infinitesimal “
\epsilon^{1}
” in textbook variational manipulations of field theories, as nothing but a stand-in for the derivative along paths (of plots) of fields. We note, however, the consistency of these formal manipulations in this infinite-dimensional setting rests crucially upon the content of Lem.
2.29
.

Outlook.

Having provided a solid foundation for synthetic constructions in field theory via infinitesimally thickened smooth sets, in the next installment of this series, we will further enlarge our probe-site inside super-commutative algebras

\mathrm{CartSp}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\mbox{-}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}\,,

to what we call super (thickened) Cartesian spaces
\mathrm{SupThCartSp}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{2}\mbox{-}\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
As we will show therein, sheaves over this site, i.e., “
super thickened smooth sets
”, provide a natural setting for a rigorous formalization of fermionic fields alongside bosonic fields and their smooth and infinitesimal structures.
See
[
Gi25
]
for an overall exposition towards that goal.

Acknowledgements.
We thank Urs Schreiber for discussions relating to various aspects of the paper, and David Jaz Myers for comments on an early version of the Appendix.

4
Appendix

4.1
The
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic nature of the Cahiers topos

In this manuscript, our aim has been to make contact with existing practice of infinitesimals in the physics literature, while using the least amount of mathematical background, but nevertheless not compromising on the level of rigor. This appendix serves to justify our usage of the
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic version of Dubuc’s “Cahiers topos” comprised of sheaves over infinitesimally thickened Cartesian probes
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, rather than the original
C^{\infty}
-algebraic version from
[
Du79
]
, via Thm.
4.7
and Cor.
4.8
. Of course, this by no means compromises the usefulness of the
C^{\infty}
-algebraic technology in more technical considerations of variants of thickened probes sites (cf. Rem.
2.18
, ftn. 3), especially in showing that these do indeed yield
“well-adapted”
models for synthetic differential geometry
[
Du79
]
[
MR91
]
[
Ko06
]
– including the case of the Cahiers topos we employ.

Lemma 4.1

(
Hadamard’s Lemma for partial derivatives).

For any smooth function
f(x,y)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})
and any
l\in\mathbbm{N}
, there exist smooth functions
\{h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{l+1}}\}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{l+1}=1,\cdots,m}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})
such that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=f(x,0)+y^{i}\cdot\partial_{y^{i}}f(x,0)+\tfrac{1}{2}y^{i}y^{j}\cdot\partial_{y^{i}}\partial_{y^{j}}f(x,0)+\cdots+\;\tfrac{1}{l!}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{l}=1}^{m}y^{i_{1}}\cdots y^{i_{l}}\cdot\partial_{y^{i_{1}}}\cdots\partial_{y^{i_{l}}}f(x,0)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\qquad+\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{l+1}=1}^{m}y^{i_{1}}\cdots y^{i_{l+1}}\cdot h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{l+1}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|\leq l}k_{I}(x)\cdot y^{I}+\sum_{|I|>l}y^{I+1}\cdot h_{I+1}(x,y)\,,

where
\{y^{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,m}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m})
are the canonical (linear) coordinate functions, and
k_{i_{1}\cdots i_{l+1}}(x)\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
are (uniquely) defined by the first equality.

Proof.

This follows by a slight generalization of the proof for the traditional Lemma by Hadamard (e.g.
[
Ne03
, Lem. 2.8]
). Consider first the case of
k,m=1
and
l=0
and define

\displaystyle g:[0,1]_{t}\times\mathbbm{R}_{x}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

\displaystyle(t,x)

\displaystyle\longmapsto f(x,t\cdot y)\,,

so that

\partial_{t}g(x,t)=\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(x,t\cdot y)\cdot y\,.

It follows that

g(x,1)-g(x,0)=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}g(x,t)\mathrm{d}t=y\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(x,t\cdot y)\mathrm{d}t\,,

and, by definition,

g(x,1)-g(x,0)\equiv f(x,y)-f(x,0)\,,

hence

f(x,y)=f(x,0)+y\cdot h(x,y)\,,

for

h(x,y):=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(x,t\cdot y)\mathrm{d}t\,.

Notice that this implies that
\partial_{y}f(x,0)=h(x,0)
, and so iterating the above on
h(x,y)
yields the claim for
m=n=1
and
l\in\mathbbm{N}
. The case of general
m,n
follows analogously.
∎

Corollary 4.2

(
Hadamard on Manifolds/Cartesian product).

Let
M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}
be a smooth manifold. For any smooth function
f\in C^{\infty}(M\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})
and any
l\in\mathbbm{N}
, there exist smooth functions
\{h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{l+1}}\}_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{l+1}=1,\cdots,m}\subset C^{\infty}(M\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})
such that

\displaystyle f(x,y)

\displaystyle=f(x,0)+y^{i}\cdot\partial_{y^{i}}f(x,0)+\tfrac{1}{2}y^{i}y^{j}\cdot\partial_{y^{i}}\partial_{y^{j}}f(x,0)+\cdots+\;\tfrac{1}{l!}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{l}=1}^{m}y^{i_{1}}\cdots y^{i_{l}}\cdot\partial_{y^{i_{1}}}\cdots\partial_{y^{i_{l}}}f(x,0)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\qquad+\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{l+1}=1}^{m}y^{i_{1}}\cdots y^{i_{l+1}}\cdot h_{i_{1}\cdots i_{l+1}}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|I|\leq l}k_{I}(x)\cdot y^{I}+\sum_{|I|>l}y^{I+1}\cdot h_{I+1}(x,y)\,,

where
\{y^{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,m}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m})
are the canonical (linear) coordinate functions, and
k_{i_{1}\cdots i_{l+1}}(x)\subset C^{\infty}(M)
are (uniquely) defined by the first equality.

Proof.

Working locally in product charts
\big\{\mathbbm{R}^{n}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}\xrightarrow{\,\,\phi_{j}\times\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}^{m}}\,\,}U_{i}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}\hookrightarrow M\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}\big\}_{j\in J}
, the result follows as above, since all of the (higher) partial derivative operators appearing are globally defined with respect to the canonical global chart on
\mathbbm{R}^{m}
, and the corresponding expansions must agree on overlaps at each polynomial order in
\{y^{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,m}
.
∎

The above implies that the function algebras of our thickened probes
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
are all, in fact, quotients of smooth Cartesian algebras
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})
.

Corollary 4.3

(
Thickened function algebras as quotient algebras).

There exist the following isomorphisms of
\mathbbm{R}
-algebras:

(i)

The function algebra of any thickened Cartesian space
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
is canonically identified with the quotient

C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\;\cong_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/(y_{1}\cdots y_{m})^{l+1}\,.

(ii)

The function algebra of any thickened Manifold
M\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthMfd}
is canonically identified with the quotient

C^{\infty}(M)\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\;\cong_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\;C^{\infty}(M\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})/(y_{1}\cdots y_{m})^{l+1}\,.

(iii)
Similarly, the corresponding thickened versions of algebras
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
and
C^{\infty}(M)
by any Weil algebra
W\cong_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)/I
may be identified with the quotients

C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W\;\cong_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/\,\tilde{I}

and

C^{\infty}(M)\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W\;\cong_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\;C^{\infty}(M\times\mathbbm{R}^{m})/\,\tilde{I}\,,

where
\tilde{I}
is the ‘lifted’
30
30

30

Any
I\subset\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
is finitely presented by polynomials in the nilpotent coordinates. These lift to polynomials in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m})
.
ideal corresponding to
I\subset\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
, so in particular
(y_{1}\cdots y_{m})^{l+1}\subset\tilde{I}
.

Proof.

The first identification follows by expanding any (representative)
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})
as

f=\sum_{|I|\leq l}k_{I}(x)\cdot y^{I}+\sum_{|I|>l}y^{I+1}\cdot h_{I+1}(x,y)\,,

by the uniqueness of the
\{k_{I}(x)\}_{|I|\leq l}
family (Lem.
4.1
). The second identification by the expansion
of representatives via Cor.
4.2
. The final identifications follow completely analogously, keeping track of the extra quotient by polynomials in
\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)
and
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})
, respectively, with respect to any identification

W\cong\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)/I
; see Lem.
2.8
.
∎

The case
(iii)
of thickened Cartesian spaces is already enough to show that they are, in fact, dual to
finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras
, i.e., dual to objects of a particularly well-behaved full subcategory of
C^{\infty}
-algebras
[
MR91
]
, and not just
\mathbbm{R}
-algebras. We shall not need to use full definition of a
C^{\infty}
-algebra
31
31

31

While an
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra structure on
A
induces, for any polynomial
p\in\mathrm{Pol}(\mathbbm{R}^{n},\,\mathbbm{R}^{m})
, a map
\widehat{p}:A^{\times n}\rightarrow A^{\times m}
compatible with the algebra multiplication, a
C^{\infty}
-algebra structure on
A
further induces such a map
\widehat{f}:A^{\times n}\rightarrow A^{\times m}
for any smooth function
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n},\mathbbm{R}^{m})
. That is, a
C^{\infty}
-algebra is nothing but a product-preserving functor
A:\mathrm{CartSp}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}
.
here, since for our purposes we may use the characterization of finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras and
C^{\infty}
-algebra morphisms between them from
[
MR91
, p. 29]
as a definition for the former:

Definition 4.4

(
Finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras).

The category
C^{\infty}_{f.g.}
-Alg of finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras is equivalent to the category whose

(i)
objects are
\mathbbm{R}
-algebras of the form

A\;\cong\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I\,,

(ii)
morphisms

\phi\;:\;A\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}B\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})/I^{\prime}

between a pair of f.g.
C^{\infty}
-algebras are given by (equivalence classes of)

\mathbbm{R}
-algebra morphisms

\psi^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\big)

(equiv. smooth
\psi:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, by Prop.
2.1
)
such that the ideal (generated by the image)
\psi^{*}(I)
is a subset of the latter target ideal
\psi^{*}(I)\subset I^{\prime}
, and where two morphisms

\psi^{*},\psi^{\prime*}:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})
are equivalent iff the maps

\big(\mathrm{pr}_{i}\circ\psi-\mathrm{pr}_{i}\circ\psi^{\prime}\big)\;\;:\;\;\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

lie in the ideal
I^{\prime}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})
for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
, where

\mathrm{pr}_{i}:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
are the canonical coordinate projection maps.

Remark 4.5

(
Determined by value on generators).

A most useful property of maps between finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras is that they are completely determined by their values on the (linear coordinate) “generators” of the source
C^{\infty}
-algebra

\big[x^{j}\big]\equiv\big[\mathrm{pr}_{j}\big]\;\longmapsto\;\phi\big(\big[x^{j}\big]\big)\equiv\big[x^{j}\circ\psi\big]\,.

Evidently, in the characterization of Def.
4.4
, this extra property is encoded by the fact that any such map descends (dually) from a smooth map of manifolds
\psi:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
. Crucially, this might not be true for
arbitrary

\mathbbm{R}
-algebra morphisms between
\mathbbm{R}
-algebras of the general form
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I
. However, as we shall show, it is true for thickened Cartesian algebras (Prop.
4.6
).

The original Cahiers topos of Dubuc
[
Du79
, §4]
[
KR87
]
is defined as sheaves over site with (Weil) thickened Cartesian spaces as the opposite of a subcategory of
C^{\infty}_{f.g.}
-Alg with respect to the (good open) coverage
32
32

32

To be precise, the original definition by Dubuc employs the site of thickened manifolds as an opposite subcategory of
C^{\infty}_{f.g.}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}
(rather than thickened Cartesian spaces), but for the given (good open) coverage the two corresponding sheaf categories are equivalent, precisely as in (
11
). Hence, we focus solely on the thickened Cartesian
C^{\infty}
-algebraic site.
as in (
6
), that is, with
C^{\infty}
-algebra morphisms rather than
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra morphisms as we have done. We denote this site by

C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\;{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\;C^{\infty}_{f.g.}\mbox{-Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\,.

In a bit more detail, we note that
C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}
enjoys a monoidal (tensor) product
\otimes_{\infty}
which is in fact a pushout construction
and, in particular, satisfies

C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\otimes_{\infty}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\;\cong\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{R})\,,

and similarly for any two manifolds
M,N\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}
.
33
33

33

By which it immediately follows that the (fully faithful) embedding
\mathrm{SmthMfd}\hookrightarrow C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{op}
preserves products (and in fact furthermore pullbacks/intersections), in contrast to that into
\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}
from Prop.
2.1
.
This is in stark contrast to the
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic tensor product
\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}
which only recovers part of the smooth function on products,
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}\times\mathbbm{R})
. The existence of the
C^{\infty}
-tensor product is one of the main motivating reasons for considering the notion of
C^{\infty}
-algebras.

This means that, in particular, when one considers the
C^{\infty}
-algebraic version of the site, the objects
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\in C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
must be given by as formal duals of

C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\infty}\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})

However, a standard result (
[
Ko06
, Thm. 5.3]
) shows that the two tensor products coincide on products with Weil algebras

C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\infty}\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\;\cong\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\,,

where the isomorphism is as sets,
\mathbbm{R}
-algebras, or even
C^{\infty}
-algebras. Hence, one may think of the two versions of the site as having the same objects but potentially different Hom-sets.

We will show that considering the notion of
C^{\infty}
-algebra morphisms is, in a sense, redundant when working with the subcategory of thickened Cartesian spaces, in that these coincide precisely with the set of
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra maps between any two finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras. Notice that by Def.
4.4
, any
C^{\infty}
-algebra map between (function algebras of) thickened Cartesian space is, in particular, an
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map, i.e., there is a canonical injection

\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D},\,\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\,{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D},\,\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\,.

Thus we only need to show that an arbitrary
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map

\phi^{*}:\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

is in fact a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map, thus making the above inclusion surjective and so a bijection.

To that end, we note that Hadamard’s Lemma
2.6
(and Lem.
4.1
) may be centered around points different from the origin, precisely as with Taylor expansions. A minor modification of the proof of Lem.
4.1
for the case of
k=0
yields that for any
y_{0}\in\mathbbm{R}^{m}
, any smooth
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m})
may be expanded as

f(y)=\sum_{|I|\leq l}k_{I}(y_{0})\cdot(y-y_{0})^{I}+\sum_{|I|>l}(y-y_{0})^{I+1}\cdot h_{I+1}(y)\,,

for some uniquely defined smooth functions
\{k_{I}\}_{|I|\leq l}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m})
. Choosing
y=y_{0}+y_{1}
then yields that for any two points
y_{0},y_{1}\in\mathbbm{R}^{m}

\displaystyle f(y_{0}+y_{1})=\sum_{|I|\leq l}k_{I}(y_{0})\cdot(y_{1})^{I}+\sum_{|I|>l}(y_{1})^{I+1}\cdot h_{I+1}(y_{0}+y_{1})\,.

(103)

Proposition 4.6

(
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra maps into thickened Cartesian algebras).

(i)
Any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map

\phi\;:\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/\,\tilde{I}

is a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map, where the latter identification is via Cor.
4.3
.

(ii)
More generally, any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map

\phi\;:\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})/{I^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/\,\tilde{I}\,,

where
I^{\prime}=(p_{1},\cdots,p_{n})
is an ideal finitely generated by polynomials, is a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map.

Proof.

Consider first the case for
k^{\prime}=1
,
I^{\prime}={0}
, and recall Cor.
4.3
that

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W

\displaystyle\;\cong\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)/\big(r_{1}(\epsilon),\cdots,r_{q}(\epsilon)\big)

\displaystyle\;\cong\;C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{y}\big)\Big/\Big\langle\!\big(y^{1},\cdots,y^{m}\big)^{l+1},\big(r_{1}(y),\cdots,r_{q}(y)\big)\!\Big\rangle

where
\{y^{i}\}_{1,\cdots,m}
are the coordinates on
\mathbbm{R}^{m}
and
\{r_{j}\}_{1,\cdots,q}
are polynomials in these coordinates. Now any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map

\phi:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\big/\big(r^{1}(\epsilon),\cdots,r^{q}(\epsilon)\big)

acts in particular on the (to be) generator of
t\equiv\mathrm{id}_{\mathbbm{R}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})

t\quad\longmapsto\;\;\Bigg[\sum_{|I|\leq l}f_{I}(x)\cdot\epsilon^{I}\Bigg]=\Bigg[\sum_{|I|\leq l}f_{I}(x)\cdot\epsilon^{I}+h_{i}(x,\epsilon)\cdot r^{i}(\epsilon)\Bigg]\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}\big(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)/\big(r^{1}(\epsilon),\cdots,r^{q}(\epsilon)\big)\,.

Notice the target element defines a smooth function

f:=\sum_{|I|\leq l}f_{I}(x)\cdot y^{I}\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthMfd}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m},\,\mathbbm{R})

which hence determines a unique
C^{\infty}\mbox{-}
algebra map

[f]^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{y}\big)\Big/\Big\langle\!\big(y^{1},\cdots,y^{m}\big)^{l+1},\big(r_{1}(y),\cdots,r_{q}(y)\big)\!\Big\rangle

via its equivalence class

\big[f\big]=\bigg[f+\sum_{|I|>l}m_{I}(x,y)\cdot y^{I}+n_{i}(x,y)\cdot r^{i}(y)\bigg].

The corresponding action may be explicitly determined, for instance, by the pullback (of any representative)

\displaystyle f^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\longmapsto g\circ f=g\bigg(f_{0}(x)+\sum_{1\leq|I|\leq l}f_{I}(x)\cdot y^{I}\bigg)

since the target may be expanded, uniquely up to
\mathcal{O}(y^{l})
for any representative,

g\big(f_{0}(x)\big)+\sum_{1\leq|J|\leq l}\widehat{k}_{J}\Big(f_{0}(x),\{f_{I}(x)\}_{1\leq|I|\leq l}\Big)\cdot y^{J}+\mathcal{O}\big(y^{l+1}\big)\,.

Here the terms
\widehat{k}_{J}\big(f_{0}(x),\{f_{I}(x)\}_{1\leq|I|\leq l}\big)
are uniquely determined by the (partial) Hadamard Lemma on sums (
103
) on
g
, together with the corresponding multinomial expansions. The latter, hence, clearly descends to the
C^{\infty}
-algebra map

[f]^{*}\;:\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{y}\big)\Big/\Big\langle\!\big(y^{1},\cdots,y^{m}\big)^{l+1},\big(r_{1}(y),\cdots,r_{q}(y)\big)\!\Big\rangle

claimed above.

Since
[f]^{*}
is an algebra map defined via the value
\phi(t)
it is immediate to see it coincides with

\phi
on the polynomial subalgebra of
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})
. To witness further that the two
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra maps coincide on all of
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})
, notice that for any
x_{0}\in\mathbbm{R}_{x}^{k}
we may compose with the evaluation algebra map

\phi_{x_{0}}:=\big(\mathrm{ev}_{x_{0}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W}\big)\circ\phi\;\;:\;\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\big)\otimes W\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow W

and the value of
\phi(g)
is completely determined by the family of evaluations
\phi_{x_{0}}(g)
, by expanding via the
\mathbbm{R}
-tensor product structure. In other words, the family
\{(\mathrm{ev}_{x_{0}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{W})\}_{x_{0}\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}}
is “jointly monic” (cf.
[
Ko06
, (8.6)]
). The same applies for
[f]^{*}
and its evaluations. But each of the
\phi_{x_{0}}
evaluation maps coincides precisely with the corresponding evaluation maps
[f]^{*}_{x_{0}}

\phi_{x_{0}}(g)=[f]^{*}_{x_{0}}(g)=g\big(f_{0}(x_{0})\big)+\sum_{1\leq|J|\leq l}\widehat{k}_{J}\Big(f_{0}(x),\{f_{I}(x)\}_{1\leq|I|\leq l}\Big)(x_{0})\cdot\epsilon^{J}\,,

since any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map into a Weil algebra is necessarily a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map, and hence completely determined by its value on the coordinate
t
(
[
MR91
, Cor. 3.19]
[
KMS93
, Step 1, prf of Thm. 35.14]
.

The case of
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra maps
\phi\,:\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W
for
k^{\prime}\geq 2
follows analogously by evaluating
\phi
on each of the (to be generators) linear functions
t^{i}=\mathrm{pr}_{i}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})
.

Lastly, consider the case of a source algebra
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})/I^{\prime}
with
I^{\prime}=(p_{1},\cdots p_{n})
where each
p_{j}=p_{j}(t^{1},\cdots,t^{k^{\prime}})
is a polynomial in the coordinates of
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}
. The proof proceeds precisely as above, by defining
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k+m}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}
via the values of
\phi
on each of the (equivalence classes of) linear coordinates
t^{i}
, so that in particular
f^{*}(t^{i})=\phi(t^{i})
. The only extra thing to show is that

f^{*}(I^{\prime})\,\subset\,\widetilde{I}\,,

so that
f^{*}
indeed (dually) descends and defines a
C^{\infty}
-algebra morphism, which by the previous argument will coincide with
\phi
on all of
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})/{I^{\prime}}
. To see this, it suffices to notice that since
I^{\prime}
is generated by
polynomials
, and
\phi
takes values in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/\widetilde{I}
, we have

p\big(\phi(t^{1}),\cdots,\phi(t^{k^{\prime}})\big)=\phi\big(p(t^{1},\cdots,t^{k^{\prime}})\big)\quad\in\quad\widetilde{I}

and hence also

\displaystyle f^{*}p:

\displaystyle=p\circ f\equiv p\big(f^{*}(t^{1}),\cdots,f^{*}(t^{k^{\prime}})\big)

\displaystyle=p\big(\phi(t^{1}),\cdots,\phi(t^{k^{\prime}})\big)=\phi\big(p(t^{1},\cdots,t^{k^{\prime}})\big)\quad\in\quad\widetilde{I}\,.

∎

Theorem 4.7

(
Thickened Cartesian spaces are smooth loci).

The categories
C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
and
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
are (strictly) equivalent

C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\,\cong\,\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\,.

Proof.

Since the two categories may be viewed as having the ‘same’ objects, it is sufficient to show that the two kinds of Hom-sets are in canonical bijection. This follows directly by
(ii)
of Prop.
4.6
together with the (polynomially finitely generated) quotient identification of thickened Cartesian algebras from Cor.
4.3
as a source algebra.
Alternatively, it can also be seen via the sequence of canonical bijections

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W^{\prime},\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W\big)\,

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}),\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W\big)\times\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(W^{\prime},\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W\big)
