\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}),\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\infty}\!W\big)\times\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\big(W^{\prime},\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W\big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})\otimes_{\infty}W^{\prime},\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W\big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W^{\prime},\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes W\big)\,,

where the first uses the colimit property of
\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}
, the second uses Prop.
4.6
(i)
and the analogous result that can be proven similarly for the source algebra being a Weil algebra (in fact with target any
C^{\infty}
-algebra, see
[
MR91
, Cor. 3.19]
[
Ko06
, Thm. 5.3]
). Finally, we use the colimit property of the
C^{\infty}
-tensor product in
C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}
, which in fact coincides with the
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic one when tensoring with Weil algebras (
[
Ko06
, Thm. 5.3]
) (hence the reason of being unspecified in the second target of the Hom set).
∎

Although we do not need to go into the trouble of proving the analogous statement for target algebras being those of thickened manifolds here, it should nevertheless follow similarly – by first identifying
C^{\infty}(M)\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I_{s(M)}
, where
s:M\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
is some smooth closed embedding (by Whitney’s embedding theorem, see e.g.
[
MR91
, p.29]
) and
I_{s(M)}
is the ideal of smooth functions that vanish along
s(M)\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k}
(cf. Lem.
4.15
).

Corollary 4.8

(
Cahiers topos is
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic).

When both categories are endowed with the good open coverage from Eq. (
6
) they are also (strictly) isomorphic as sites. It follows that the Cahiers topos of Dubuc from
[
Du79
]
is equivalent to the topos of thickened smooth sets from Def.
2.11

\mathrm{Sh}(C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp})\,\cong\,\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Remark 4.9

(
Preservation of products).

By virtue of the Cahiers topos being a well-adapted model for synthetic differential geometry
[
Du79
]
[
MR91
]
[
Ko06
]
, the embedding

y\,:\,\mathrm{SmthMfd}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}

preserves, in particular, products.
34
34

34

In fact, it also preserves transverse pullbacks (intersections).
Notice, this is not immediate from the
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic incarnation of the (dual) site, if considered with its
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic monoidal structure, but it follows immediately if instead supplied with the
C^{\infty}
-algebraic monoidal structure.

4.2
Embedding manifolds with corners and their Weil bundles

Embedding manifolds with corners

It has been shown in
[
Re07
]
, and then streamlined in
[
Ko06
, Thm. 9.6]
, that smooth (second countable and Hausdorff) manifolds with boundary embed fully faithfully in the standard
C^{\infty}
-algebraic version of Cahiers topos via the restricted Yoneda functor

\displaystyle y\,:\,\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{bnd}}

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Sh}(C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp})

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\big(C^{\infty}(X),\,-\big)\,.

Under the equivalence of Cor.
4.8
, this same functor is trivially transported to yield a fully faithful embedding in our
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic topos.
Notice, however, that it is a priori still using
C^{\infty}
-algebra morphisms. Nevertheless, by our
Cor.
4.16

below, these may be taken to be
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic morphisms since these, again, are actually exhausted by
C^{\infty}
-algebraic maps out of manifold-with-boundary algebras. In fact, below we present a straightforward extension of this result to include smooth manifolds with corners.

We begin by recalling the standard but crucial result, often referred to as “Milnor’s exercise” (see, e.g.,
[
KMS93
, §35.9]
).

Proposition 4.10

(
Milnor’s exercise).

The evaluation map of sets

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}\,:\,|M|\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,\mathbbm{R}\big),

where
|M|
is the underlying set of a smooth (second countable and Hausdorff) manifold
M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}
, is a bijection.

Let us easily extend this classical result to any
closed
subset
|K|\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, viewed as the formal dual smooth space

K\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}

of the function algebra

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(K)\,:

\displaystyle=\big\{f:|K|\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\;|\;\exists\,\widetilde{f}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\mathrm{\,\,such\,\,that\,\,}\widetilde{f}|_{K}=f\big\}\;\cong\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I_{K}\,,

(104)

where
I_{K}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
is the ideal of smooth function on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
which vanish on
|K|\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}
.

Corollary 4.11

(
Milnor’s exercise for smooth closed subspaces).

The evaluation map of sets

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}\,:\,|K|\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(K),\,\mathbbm{R}\big),

for any closed subspace
K\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}
is a bijection.

Proof.

Using the quotient map
\tau:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I_{K}\,\cong\,C^{\infty}(K)
, any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra morphism
\phi:C^{\infty}(K)\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
pulls back to a morphism

\widetilde{\phi}\,:=\,\phi\circ\tau\;\;:\;\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,.

By the original result of “Milnor’s exercise” Prop.
4.10
, this corresponds uniquely to evaluating at a point
p\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, so that

\phi(f)=:\widetilde{\phi}(\widetilde{f})\,=\,f(p)

where
\widetilde{f}|_{K}=f
.

All that remains is to show that
p
is actually a point within
K
, i.e.,
p\in|K|
.
Towards contradiction, assume instead
p\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}-K
, and take any
g\in I_{K}
such that
g(p)\neq 0
. That is, any smooth function
g:\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
which vanishes on the closed subspace K and is non-vanishing on a point
p
outside
K

(which exist, e.g., via bump functions). Then we have

\widetilde{\phi}(g)=\phi\circ\tau(g)=\phi(0)=0

but also

\widetilde{\phi}(p)=g(p)\neq 0\,,

which shows that, necessarily,
p\in|K|
.
∎

Under the Whitney’s embedding theorem and its (non-trivial) generalizations to include boundaries and corners
(see e.g.

[
Jo19
, Prop. 3.1]
[
Me96
, Prop. 1.15.1]
[
MROD92
, Cor. 11.3.10]
), the above result further implies an analogous statement for arbitrary smooth (second countable and Hausdorff) manifolds with corners.

Corollary 4.12

(
Milnor’s exercise for manifolds with corners).

The evaluation map of sets

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}\,:\,|M|\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,\mathbbm{R}\big),

for any smooth (second countable and Hausdorff) manifold with corners
M
, is a bijection.

Proof.

By Whitney’s embedding theorem and its generalizations, for any
k^{\prime}
-dimensional manifold with corners
M
, there exists a smooth closed embedding

s\,:\,M{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathbbm{R}^{k},

into a Cartesian space of some dimension
k\geq k^{\prime}
,
hence yielding a homeomorphism onto its closed image

M\,\cong_{\mathrm{Top}}\,s(M){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 16.38895pt}}\mathbbm{R}^{k}\,,

which moreover satisfies

C^{\infty}(M)\,\cong_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\,C^{\infty}\big(s(M)\big)\,\cong\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})/I_{s(M)}\,.

In the formal dual sense of smooth spaces in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, this identifies any manifold with corners with some (smooth) closed subspace of a Cartesian space, and so the result follows by Cor.
4.11
.
∎

Should one wish to avoid using the non-trivial embedding result, and given the immediate validity of the result for the (closed) model cornered space
35
35

35

Generalizing the quadrant
\mathbbm{R}^{0,2}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{2}
, its 3-dimensional extension
\mathbbm{R}^{1,2}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{3}
, higher dimensional orthants
\mathbbm{R}^{0,l}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{l}
etc.

\mathbbm{R}^{k,l}:=\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times[0,\infty)^{\times l}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 16.38895pt}}\mathbbm{R}^{n}

via Cor.
4.11
, we expect also the direct proof for manifolds without boundary/corners
[
KMS93
, §35.9]
to hold essentially verbatim in the cornered setting. Importantly, however, the existence of closed embeddings
M\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
for manifolds with corners
implies, in particular, that these qualify as
finitely generated

C^{\infty}
-algebras in the sense of Def.
4.4
.

The above extension of Milnor’s exercise can be used to exhibit the fully faithful embedding of manifolds with corners into the opposite category of
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Proposition 4.13

(
Manifolds with corners embed into algebras).

The functor

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(-)\;:\;\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}

\displaystyle\;{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\;\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{op}

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\;{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\;C^{\infty}(M)\,,

sending a finite-dimensional smooth (second countable and Hausdorff) manifold with corners to its function algebra is fully faithful, in that for any pair
N,M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}
the smooth functions
f:N\xrightarrow{\;}M
biject onto the algebra homomorphisms
f^{\ast}:C^{\infty}(M)\xrightarrow{\;}C^{\infty}(N)
.

Proof.

Given Milnor’s exercise for manifolds with corners (Cor.
4.12
), the result follows essentially the same proof as in the case of no corners and boundaries, following e.g.
[
KMS93
, §35.10]
. We carefully expand these details for completeness.

Fix some
N,M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}
. To see that the functor is faithful (i.e., injective on hom-sets), consider the pullback map corresponding to a smooth map of manifolds with corners
f:N\rightarrow M
,

f^{*}\,:\,C^{\infty}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(N)

and its compositions with the point evaluations

\big\{ev_{n}\circ f^{*}\,:\,C^{\infty}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(N)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\big\}_{n\in N}\,.

By Cor.
4.12
, the latter set is in bijection with a subset of points in
|M|
, namely

\mathrm{Im}(f)\;\;\subset\;\;|M|

via
\mathrm{ev}_{n}\circ f^{*}\mapstochar\rightarrow f(n)
. Hence for any other
smooth
map
g:N\rightarrow M
such that
g^{*}=f^{*}:C^{\infty}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(N)
, it follows that pointwise

g(n)\,=\,f(n)

which shows that the functor is faithful. More categorically, this can be succinctly expressed via the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}
\textstyle{\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{|-|}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Set}\,,}

where
\Gamma
is the functor that extracts the underlying set of “
\mathbbm{R}
-points” as
\Gamma(A):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}(A,\,\mathbbm{R})
. Since, the forgetful functor
|-|:\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}
is faithful, it follows that
C^{\infty}(-)
is also necessarily faithful.

To see that the functor is full (i.e., surjective on Hom-sets), consider an arbitrary morphism of algebras

\phi\,:\,C^{\infty}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(N)

and the collection of compositions with the evaluation maps

\big\{ev_{n}\circ\phi\,:\,C^{\infty}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(N)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\big\}_{n\in N}\,.

As above, by Cor.
4.12
, each element of this set is an algebra morphism
C^{\infty}(M)\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
and hence uniquely corresponds to some evaluation map on
|M|

\mathrm{ev}_{n}\circ\phi\longmapsto\mathrm{ev}_{f(n)}\,.

In other words, this determines a map of sets

f\,:\,|N|\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow|M|

which, by construction, satisfies

f^{*}\,=\,\phi
.

It remains to check that the latter map is actually
smooth
as a map of smooth manifolds with corners. Towards this, notice that its postcomposition
with any smooth function
\sigma\in C^{\infty}(M)
is smooth, since

\sigma\circ f=f^{*}\sigma=\phi(\sigma)\;\;\in\;\;C^{\infty}(N).

Fix a point
n\in N
take
\xi:V_{f(n)}\subset M\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times[0,\infty)^{\times{l}}=:\mathbbm{R}^{k,l}
to be a local chart (with corners) around
f(n)\in M
. Consider the (local) coordinate projection maps

x^{i}\,=\,\pi^{i}\circ\xi\;\;:\;\;V_{f(n)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k,l}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

of which those with
i>k
actually take values in the non-negative numbers
[0,\infty)\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}
.

Choosing a smooth, non-negative, bump function whose value is
1
in some smaller neighborhood around
f(n)\subset V_{f(n)}
, which also exists on second countable and Hausdorff manifolds
with corners

(see, e.g.,
[
Haj14
, Lem. 19]
), there exist a
smooth
global extension of each of the coordinates

\widetilde{x}^{\,i}\;\;\in\;\;C^{\infty}(M)\,,

so that also

\widetilde{x}^{\,i}\circ f=\phi\big(\widetilde{x}^{i}\big)\;\;\in\;\;C^{\infty}(N)

is smooth. Thus, for any local chart around
\psi^{-1}:U_{n}\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\rightarrow N
around
n\in N
, it follows that the composition

\big(\,\widetilde{x}^{\,i}\circ f\big)\circ\psi^{-1}\;\;:\;\;U_{n}\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow N\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

is a smooth map, even for any
i>k
as a map into
[0,\infty)
.
But this says that the map

\xi\circ f\circ\psi^{-1}=\big(x^{1},\cdots,x^{k},x^{k+1},\cdots x^{k+l}\big)\circ f\circ\psi^{-1}\quad:\quad U_{n}\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow N\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k,l}

is smooth. Since this holds for all points
n\in N
and local charts around it and its image, the map
f
is smooth as a map of manifolds with corners.
∎

The Yoneda embedding then immediately implies that smooth manifolds with corners embed fully faithfully as representable objects in the category of (pre)sheaves on
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
. In fact, one can show that these remain “representable from outside” when the probes are restricted to thickened Cartesian spaces
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
. The proof of this result follows along the lines of that from
[
Ko06
, Thm. 9.6]
which treats the case of manifolds with boundary (albeit having in mind
C^{\infty}
-algebraic morphisms, which is actually not necessary), with some extra bells and whistles.

Theorem 4.14

(
Manifolds with corners embed into the Cahiers topos).

The restricted Yoneda embedding

\displaystyle y\,:\,\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,\mathcal{O}(-)\big)|_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}

embeds smooth (second countable and Hausdorff) manifolds with corners fully faithfully in thickened smooth sets.

Proof.

Firstly, the sheaf condition is satisfied since the value of the presheaf
y(M)
on each thickened Cartesian space,
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
, defines a (topological/petit) sheaf on each
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\in\mathrm{CartSp}
, for all infinitesimal points
\mathbbm{D}
(Rem.
2.12
). To see the latter, consider any closed smooth embedding
s:M\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, so that
C^{\infty}(M)\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})/I_{s(M)}
. Postcomposing any family of compatible maps

\{\phi_{i}:U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\}_{i\in I}

over an open cover
\{U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\}_{i\in I}
with the closed embedding
s:M\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
, we get a family of compatible maps into the Cartesian space
\mathbbm{R}^{n}

\big\{s\circ\phi_{i}:U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}\big\}_{i\in I}\,.

By the sheaf condition on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, these glue to a unique map (a unique
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot)

\phi_{s}\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\,.

We still need to show that this map actually factors through
M
, that is, within
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
. To see this,
take any
f\in I_{s(M)}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\cong\mathrm{hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(y(\mathbbm{R}^{n}),\,y(\mathbbm{R})\big)
, so that

f\circ\phi_{s}\quad\in\quad\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}\big(y(\mathbbm{R}^{n}\times\mathbbm{D}),\,y(\mathbbm{R})\big)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}(\mathbbm{R}^{n}\times\mathbbm{D},\mathbbm{R})

with the property that the restrictions factor (dually in CAlg
R
) through
M

f\circ\phi_{s}|_{U_{i}}=f\circ s\circ\phi_{i}\;\;:\;\;U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,,

i.e.,

f\circ\phi_{s}|_{U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D}}\,=\,0\,\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}(U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D},\mathbbm{R})\,.

for each element
U_{i}\times\mathbbm{D}
of the cover. Thus, by the petit sheaf property of
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D},\mathbbm{R})
, or equivalently the gros sheaf property of
y(\mathbbm{R})
, the glued map also necessarily vanishes. i.e.

f\circ\phi_{s}\,=\,0\,.

Faithfulness follows analogously to that of Prop.
4.13
. Namely, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Gamma}
\textstyle{\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y}
\scriptstyle{|-|}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Set}\,,}

where
\Gamma:=(-)(*)
is the functor that extracts the underlying set of
*
-plots
\Gamma(\mathcal{F}):=\mathcal{F}(*)
. Since, the forgetful functor
|-|:\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}
is faithful, it follows that
y
is also necessarily faithful.

It remains to show that the functor is full. Consider then an arbitrary map of thickened smooth sets

\mathcal{H}\,:\,y(N)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(M)\,,

where
N,M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}
are any two manifolds with corners. This encodes, in particular, a map on the underlying sets of
*
-plots

h:=\mathcal{H}(*)\equiv\Gamma(\mathcal{H})\;\;:\;\;|N|\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow|M|\,,

which we wish to show qualifies as a smooth map
h:N\rightarrow M
with respect to their manifold-with-corners structures. Since there exists a (smooth) embedding
M\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
, and a map into
M
is smooth only if it locally extends to a smooth map in the corresponding ambient model Cartesian space
\mathbbm{R}^{k,l}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k+l}
, it is sufficient to show the map
h
is smooth for the case of
M=\mathbbm{R}^{n}
. But a map
N\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
is smooth if and only if each projection
N\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}\xrightarrow{\pi^{i}}\mathbbm{R}
is smooth, hence it is in fact sufficient to show the result for the case of
M=\mathbbm{R}
.

Now, by precomposing with any map of thickened smooth sets

\mathcal{G}\;:\;y(\mathbbm{R}^{d})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(N)\,,

we obtain a map in
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}

\mathcal{H}\circ\mathcal{G}\;\;:\;\;y(\mathbbm{R}^{d})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(N)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R})

which by the (original) fully faithfulness of the Yoneda embedding over
\mathrm{CartSp}
from (
10
), corresponds to a
smooth map

h\circ g\equiv\Gamma(\mathcal{G}\circ\mathcal{H})\equiv\mathcal{G}(*)\circ\mathcal{H}(*)\;\;:\;\;\mathbbm{R}^{d}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}\,.

In words, the precomposition of
h:|N|\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
with
any
smooth map
g:\mathbbm{R}^{d}\rightarrow N
is smooth.

The latter statement is sufficient to show that
h
is smooth as a map of manifolds with corners
N\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
. This is a local (chart-wise) statement in
N
, and hence follows immediately for any
n\in\mathrm{Int}(N)
which is necessarily contained in a local Cartesian chart without corners
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
. Lastly, consider the case where
p
is instead any point of the corner/boundary set subset, together with an adapted local chart
\phi:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\rightarrow N
centered at
n
,

\phi\big(\,\underline{a}_{k^{\prime}}\,,\,\underline{0}_{l^{\prime}}\big)\,=\,n\,,

and consider the corresponding smooth
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
-plot given by (precomposing with)

\displaystyle s\quad:\quad\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\qquad

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\qquad\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}

(105)

\displaystyle\Big(x^{1},\cdots,x^{k^{\prime}},x^{k^{\prime}+1},\cdots,x^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\Big(\,x^{1},\cdots,\,x^{k^{\prime}},\big(x^{k^{\prime}+1}\big)^{2},\cdots\big(x^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\big)^{2}\,\Big)\,.

(106)

This yields the
smooth
map

h\circ\phi\circ s\;\;:\;\;\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

which is
even
in (each of) the last
l
-coordinates, i.e.,

h\circ\phi\circ s\,\big(x^{1},\cdots,x^{k^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}x^{k^{\prime}+1},\cdots,x^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\big)\,=\,h\circ\phi\circ s\,\big(x^{1},\cdots,x^{k^{\prime}},x^{k^{\prime}+1},\cdots,x^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\big)\,,

and similarly for the rest of the final
l^{\prime}-1
coordinates. By the symmetry of the above function, there exists
36
36

36

For the case of the half line
\mathbbm{R}^{1+0}=\mathbbm{R}^{1}
, this is the classic result of
[
Wh92
]
. The generalization to smooth functions
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
, which are even in each of the last
l^{\prime}
-coordinates, follows easily by applying the original result on each of these variables, inductively, via the use of the partial Hadamard Lemma
4.1
. Alternatively, the existence of such a function is also guaranteed by the more general result of
[
Schw75
]
applied to the case of the group action
(\mathbbm{Z}_{2})^{\times l^{\prime}}
on
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
acting by reflection on the
l^{\prime}
-last coordinates.
a
smooth
extension

h_{\phi}\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}

such that

h_{\phi}|_{\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}}\,=\,h\circ\phi\,,

by which it follows that
h
is smooth as a map of manifolds with corners.

It remains to show that the induced map of thickened smooth manifolds agrees with the original map
\mathcal{H}
, namely,

y(h)\,\equiv\,\mathcal{H}\,.

On
*
-plots, these necessarily agree by definition of
h:=\mathcal{H}(*)
. Thus it suffices to prove that
j:=y(h)-\mathcal{H}
is the zero map of thickened smooth sets, given that
j(*):N\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
is the zero map of sets. This is the content of
[
Ko06
, Lem. 9.9]
, which applies almost verbatim in the case of manifolds with corners, noting that the corresponding
C^{\infty}
-algebra morphisms mentioned therein are exhausted by
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra morphisms.

The outline of this fact is as follows: it suffices to show that the composition of any plot

y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(N)\xlongrightarrow{j}y(\mathbbm{R})

is the zero map.
But such a composition, by the Yoneda embedding, is exactly a “pullback”

\displaystyle\gamma:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\longmapsto\gamma(t)

which is completely determined by its value on the generator, by Prop.
4.6
. Thus, it must be that
\gamma(t)=0
, which in turn is true if and only if the compositions with evaluation maps

\mathrm{ev}_{p}\circ\gamma\,:\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\,,

for all
p\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, i.e.,
\gamma(t)(p)=0
for all
p\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, by the jointly monic property of Weil algebras. This reduces the problem to showing that all compositions

y(\mathbbm{D})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow y(N)\xlongrightarrow{j}y(\mathbbm{R})

vanish. The first map corresponds, by the Yoneda Lemma and the definition of
y(N)
, to an algebra morphism
C^{\infty}(N)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
, whose value depends only on the jets
j_{n}^{\infty}f
of each function
f\in C^{\infty}(N)
at
n:*\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow N
. That is, such algebra maps factor through the corresponding jet algebras
J^{\infty}_{n}N
, or dually through the infinitesimal neighborhoods (Def.
2.36
, Lem.
2.40
) at
n\in N

y(\mathbbm{D})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{D}_{n,N}\hookrightarrow N\xlongrightarrow{j}\mathbbm{R}\,.

Since infinitesimal neighborhoods always lie in such local chart
\mathbbm{D}_{n,N}\hookrightarrow U_{n}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
, it suffices to prove it for the case of the trivial manifold
\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
. This final statement follows as in the case of
\mathbbm{R}^{n,1}
from
[
Ko06
, Lem. 9.9]
, by essentially cosmetic (i.e., numerical) changes and using instead the corresponding squaring map from (
105
).
∎

Let us close this section with a result that further supports the choice of
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra morphism in the definition of the embedding
y:\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
from Thm.
4.14
, rather the traditional
C^{\infty}
-algebraic approach.

Lemma 4.15

(
Plots of smooth closed subspaces).

Let
K\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
be a closed (smooth) subspace defined by its algebra of smooth functions
C^{\infty}(K)\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})/I_{K}
as in (
104
), which further satisfies
\mathrm{Cl}(\mathrm{Int}K)=K
. Then any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map

\phi\,:\,C^{\infty}(K)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/\,\tilde{I}

is a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map.

Proof.

We need to show that any such algebra map
\phi
is given by the (equivalence class of a) pullback of a smooth map
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k+m}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
as per Def.
4.4
, hence extending the result of Prop.
4.6
.
Consider first the case of the target algebra being the non-thickened Cartesian algebra
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
, which already contains the skeleton of the argument. Under the quotient projection

\displaystyle\tau:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})/I_{K}\,\cong\,C^{\infty}(K)\,,

(107)

this maps the equivalence classes of the linear coordinate functions (projections)
\{[t^{i}]\}_{i=1,...,n}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})/I_{K}

to
n
distinct functions
\{f^{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,n}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
,
i.e.,

f^{i}\,:=\,\phi\big([t^{i}]\big)\;\in\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,.

In other words, precomposing
\phi
with the quotient projection
we get a map

\phi\circ\tau\;\;:\;\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})/I_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})

which sends each of generators
t^{i}
to
f^{i}
.

By the embedding of Prop.
4.13
, this uniquely corresponds to the smooth map

f=(f^{1},\cdots,f^{n})\;\;:\;\;\mathbbm{R}^{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}

such that
\phi\circ\tau=f^{*}
. We claim that

f^{*}(I_{K})\,\subset\,\{0\}\;{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt}}\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,,

that is, for all
g\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})
with
g|_{K}=0
,

g\circ f\,=\,0\;\;\in\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,.

Since this must be the case for all such smooth
g
, whose collection can attain arbitrary values along the open subset
\mathbbm{R}^{n}-K\subset\mathbbm{R}^{n}
(by using bump functions), this holds if and only if

f(x)\in K\quad\forall\quad x\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}\,.

Hence towards contradiction, assume otherwise, namely
\exists\,x_{0}\,\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}
such that

f(x_{0})\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}-K\,.

Then take some
g\in I_{K}
which is non-vanishing at
f(x_{0})\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}-K
(via bump functions), so that

f^{*}g(x_{0})=g\big(f(x_{0})\big)\neq 0\,.

On the other hand, by construction
f^{*}=\phi\circ\tau
, so that

f^{*}=\phi\circ\tau(g)=\phi([0])=0\,,

i.e., a contradiction. Hence there cannot exist such
x_{0}
with
f(x_{0})\in\mathbbm{R}^{n}-K
, and the map
\phi
is
necessarily

C^{\infty}
-algebraic.
37
37

37

Notice, this encodes the consistency of the intuition that a “smooth map”
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow K
in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, defined dually as an algebra map
\phi:C^{\infty}(K)\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})
should correspond to a smooth map (of manifolds)
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
that factors (point-wise) through the closed subspace
|K|\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
.

Next consider the case of the target function algebra being
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1))\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}_{y})/(y^{2})
. Precomposing the algebra morphism with (
107
) and going through the same steps as above we get a map

\phi\circ\tau\;\;:\;\;C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})/I_{K}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}^{y}\big)/\big(y^{2}\big)\,,

which defines
n
elements in the target algebra by the value of the generators

\big[f^{i}=f^{i}_{0}(x)+f^{i}_{1}(x)\cdot y\big]\,:=\,\phi\circ\tau\big(t^{i}\big)=\phi\big([t^{i}]\big)\quad\in\quad C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}_{y}\big)/(y^{2})\,.

Proceeding as in the proof of Prop.
4.6
, these define a smooth function

f=(f^{1},\cdots,f^{n})\;\;:\;\;\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}_{y}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n},

such that
\phi\circ\tau=f^{*}
. This displays
\phi
as a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map if

f^{*}(I_{K})\subset(y^{2}){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt}}C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}_{y}\big)\,,

i.e., for all
g\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n})
with
g|_{K}=0
,

g\circ f=y^{2}\cdot h_{g}(x,y)\;\;\in\;\;C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}^{y}\big)\,.

Expanding the left-hand side using Hadamard’s Lemma on sums (
103
),

g\big(f_{0}(x)+f_{1}(x)\cdot y)\,=\,g\big(f_{0}(x)\big)+\sum_{i}\partial_{t^{i}}g\big(f_{0}(x)\big)\cdot f_{1}^{i}(x)\cdot y+\mathcal{O}(y^{2})\,,

which is thus of order
y^{2}
if and only if both

g\circ f_{0}\,=\,0\,\quad\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\quad\sum_{i}(\partial_{t^{i}}g\circ f_{0})\cdot f^{i}_{1}\,=\,0\;\;\in\;\;C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\big)\,.

By the proof of the non-thickened case, the former condition holds for all such
g
if and only if the image of
f_{0}
lies in
K
,

f_{0}(x)\in K\quad\forall\quad x\in\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\,.

Now, by the assumed smoothness of all such
g
, these extend smoothly to outside the
closed
subspace
K\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n}
while vanishing on
K
. By the assumption that
\mathrm{Cl}(\mathrm{Int}K)=K
, this automatically guarantees also that

\partial_{t}g(k)\,=\,0\quad\forall\quad k\in K\,.

Argueing again by contradiction, one sees that there cannot exist
x_{0}
such that
f(x_{0})\in\mathbbm{R}^{n}-K
, so that
\phi
is again necessarily
C^{\infty}
-algebraic.

The case of an arbitrary thickening Weil algebra
W\cong\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l))/I
follows similarly, since the corresponding higher derivatives appearing in the corresponding higher order Hadamard expansion all vanish for any smooth function
g
which vanishes along
K
.
∎

Applying Lem.
4.15
for the closed subspace
K=\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
yields the following.

Corollary 4.16

(
Plots of cornered spaces).

Let
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}:=\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times[0,\infty)^{l^{\prime}}
be any higher dimensional cornered model space, thought of as the (formal dual) smooth closed subspace of
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
as per (
104
) with smooth functions defined by

C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\big):=C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}\big)/I_{\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}},

where
I_{\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}})
is the ideal of smooth function on
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}
which vanish on
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\subset\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}+l^{\prime}}
. Then any
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra map

\phi\;:\;C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime},l^{\prime}}\big)\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbbm{R}}W\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k+m})/\,\tilde{I}

is a
C^{\infty}
-algebra map.

Weil bundles of manifolds with corners

Weil bundles of manifolds originate in the work of A. Weil
[
We53
]
(see also
[
Kol08
]
for a modern recount), who showed that a natural smooth fiber bundle structure exists on the set of algebra morphisms

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,W\big)\,,

for each Weil algebra W, where
M
is a smooth manifold without boundary. Equivalently interpreted in our context, this construction yields a smooth manifold structure on the set of formal dual maps from any infinitesimal point
\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
with
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})=W

T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\,:=\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathbbm{D},\,M)\,,

to be thought of as the smooth bundle of “
\mathbbm{D}
-shaped infinitesimal points” in
M
.
Moreover, it was shown by Dubuc
[
Du79
]
that this smooth fiber bundle structure is naturally isomorphic, under the Yoneda embedding into the Cahiers topos, to one obtained via the internal mapping space construction of thickened smooth sets (Def.
2.19
), the “
synthetic Weil bundle
”

y(T_{\mathbbm{D}}M)\,\cong\,[\mathbbm{D},\,M]\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Both the traditional Weil bundle construction and the above isomorphism have been extended to the case of manifolds with boundary in
[
Re07
]
.

Here, we briefly review the above in a manner that directly extends to the case of manifolds with corners, while remaining manifestly in the purely
\mathbbm{R}
-algebraic setting.
Towards this goal, letting
\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n+k}
be the cornered (smooth) model space, viewed as the dual of its algebra of smooth functions (Prop.
4.13
)

\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{CAlg}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}\,,

the following is directly established.

Lemma 4.17

(
Weil bundle of cornered space).

For any infinitesimal point
\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, dual to a Weil algebra
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\cong W\cong\mathbbm{R}\oplus V
, there is a canonical bijection of sets

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\,:=\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathbbm{D},\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})\;\cong\;\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times V^{\times(n+k)}\,.

It follows that any choice of basis for
V\hookrightarrow W
determines a bijection

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}\cong\mathbbm{R}^{n+(w-1)(n+k)}\times[0,\infty)^{\times k}\,,

where
w
is the dimension of
W
, hence supplying the former with a structure a (trivial) smooth manifold with corners, which further exhibits
T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
as a (trivial) smooth fiber bundle over
\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\,.

Proof.

A map
\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
corresponds to an algebra morphism

\phi:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m,k})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\cong W\,,

which is in turn completely determined by its value on the coordinate functions
\{t^{i}\}_{i=1,\cdots,n+k}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})
(Cor.
4.16
,
Lem.
4.15
)

\big\{\phi(t^{i})\big\}_{i=1,\cdots,n+k}\;\;\subset\;\;W\cong\mathbbm{R}\oplus V\,.

Post-composing these with the projection onto
\mathbbm{R}\hookrightarrow W
corresponds uniquely (dually) to evaluation at a point

p_{\phi}\,\in\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\,,

while no further conditions are imposed on the components taking values in
V\hookrightarrow W
. This yields the first (canonical) isomorphism
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathbbm{D},\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times V^{n+k}

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big(p_{\phi},\,\big(\phi(t^{i})|_{V}\big)\big)\,.

Next, a choice of a basis for
V
amounts to an isomorphism
V\cong\mathbbm{R}^{w-1}
which then further yields a particular bijection of the former Hom-set with

\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}\,.

Finally, it is immediate to see that restriction along
*\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{D}
corresponds precisely to the canonical (smooth) projection

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\,,

exhibiting the former as a (trivial) smooth fiber bundle with corners over
\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
.
∎

Moreover, the Weil bundle construction is functorial with respect to smooth maps between cornered spaces.

Lemma 4.18

(
Weil bundle functoriality).

For any smooth map

f\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}\,,

equivalently determined by the pullback
f^{*}:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m,l})\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})
(Prop.
4.13
),
composition with the elements of the corresponding Weil bundles yields a smooth bundle map
over
f
,

\displaystyle T_{\mathbbm{D}}f\,:\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}\,

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi\circ f^{*}\,.

Proof.

The map is well-defined as a map of sets,
hence the only thing to check is its smoothness under the smooth structures from Lem.
4.17
. Notice,
T_{\mathbbm{D}}f
is given by sending an element
\phi\in T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
determined by

\big\{\phi(t^{i})\big\}_{i=1,\cdots,n+k}\;\;\subset\;\;W\cong\mathbbm{R}\oplus V

to the element
T_{\mathbbm{D}}f(\phi)\in T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}
determined by

\big\{\phi\big(f^{*}(\hat{t}^{j})\big)\equiv\phi(f^{j})\big\}_{j=1,\cdots,m+l}\;\;\subset\;\;W\cong\mathbbm{R}\oplus V

where
\{\,\hat{t}^{j}\}_{j=1,\cdots,m+l}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{m,l})
are the corresponding canonical (smooth) coordinate functions and
f=(f^{1},\cdots,f^{m},\cdots,f^{m+l}):\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}\,.

Smoothness of
T_{\mathbbm{D}}f
then easily follows since each
f^{j}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})
is smooth, by a further application of Hadamard’s lemma (Lem.
2.6
). Explicitly, under some choice of basis for
W
, we have a corresponding (global) chart on
T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}

\displaystyle\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\sim\hskip 8.19447pt}\;\;T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}

\displaystyle\big(x^{i}_{0},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x^{i}_{w-1}\big)

\displaystyle\;\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\phi_{(x^{i}_{0},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x^{i}_{w-1})}\,,

where
\phi_{(x^{i}_{0},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x^{i}_{w-1})}:C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})\rightarrow W
is the algebra morphism determined by
\phi(t^{i})=(x_{0}^{i},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x_{w-1}^{i})
. Similarly,
postcomposing with
T_{\mathbbm{D}}f
results into the map

\displaystyle\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\quad T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}

\displaystyle\big(x^{i}_{0},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x^{i}_{w-1}\big)

\displaystyle\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\Big(\widehat{\phi}\,\,\big|\,\,\widehat{\phi}\big(\hat{t}^{j}\big)=\phi\big(f^{j}(t^{1},\cdots,t^{k})\big)\!\Big)\,.

Finally, postcomposing with the (inverse of the) corresponding global chart on
T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}
yields a map of cornered spaces

\displaystyle\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathbbm{R}^{m,l}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(m+l)}

whose value is on a point
(x^{i}_{0},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x^{i}_{w-1})
of the domain is uniquely determined by the components of
\widehat{\phi}(\hat{t}^{j})=\phi\big(f^{j}(t^{1},\cdots,t^{k})\big)
, obtained by first expanding each
f^{j}(t^{1},\cdots,t^{k})
up the nilpotency order of
W
(cf. Lem.
4.15
), uniquely via Hadamard’s lemma
2.6
, and then using the algebra morphism property of
\phi
. The resulting components of the map will necessarily be polynomial in the variables
(x^{i}_{0},\,x_{1}^{i},\cdots,\,x^{i}_{w-1})
, and hence smooth.
∎

Proposition 4.19

(
Weil bundle of manifold with corners).

Let
M\in\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}
be a manifold with corners modelled on
\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n+k}
.

(i)

For any infinitesimal point
\mathbbm{D}
with
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\cong W
, there exists a natural manifold-with-corner structure on

T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\,:=\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathbbm{D},\,M)

modelled on
\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}
. Under the latter manifold structure, restriction along inclusion
*\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{D}
corresponds to a canonical projection

T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow M\,,

exhibiting
T_{\mathbbm{D}}M
as a (locally trivial) fiber bundle with corners over
M
.

(ii)

Moreover, this construction forms a functor on the category of manifolds with corners

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\,:\,\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}\,.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we consider the case of
M
being covered by two charts
\psi_{1/2}:\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}_{\tiny 1/2}\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}U_{1/2}\hookrightarrow M
with
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\emptyset
. By definition of smooth functions on
M
, these induce pullback isomorphisms on local function algebras
\psi_{1/2}^{*}:C^{\infty}(U_{1/2})\xrightarrow{\sim}C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}_{1/2}\big)
.

Now, any
\phi\in T_{\mathbbm{D}}M:=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}(\mathbbm{D},\,M)\equiv\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,W\big)
centered at
p:*\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow M
, w.l.o.g say with
p\in U_{1}
, acts as some particular (higher-order) differential operator on smooth functions
h\in C^{\infty}(M)
(cf. Lem.
2.2
, Rem.
2.23
(i)
), evaluated at
p\in M
. It follows that
\phi(h)
depends only on the corresponding jets
j_{p}^{\infty}h
, i.e., factoring through the infinitesimal neighborhood
\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\hookrightarrow M
and hence further through the open submanifold
U_{1}\hookrightarrow M
. Thus, by the (topological/petit) sheaf property of
C^{\infty}(M)
on
|M|\in\mathrm{Top}
, it follows that as sets

T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\,\cong_{\mathrm{Set}}\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}U_{1}\cup T_{\mathbbm{D}}U_{2}\,.

Fix some basis for
W\cong\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})
and hence a bijection
T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\,\cong\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}
(Lem.
4.17
). Under the Weil-bundle construction, the isomorphisms
\psi_{1/2}:\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}_{1/2}\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}U_{1/2}
further yield bijections

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\big(\psi^{-1}_{1/2}\big)\,:\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}U_{1/2}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\,\cong\,\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}\,,

which serve as canonical charts on
T_{\mathbbm{D}}M
. The remaining task is to show that the transition functions are smooth, namely that over overaps
U_{12}=U_{1}\cap U_{2}
, the composition

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\big(\psi^{-1}_{1}\big)\circ T_{\mathbbm{D}}\psi_{2}\quad:\quad B_{2}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}\cong T_{\mathbbm{D}}B_{2}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}U_{12}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}B_{1}\cong B_{1}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}

where
B_{2}:=\psi^{-1}_{2}(U_{12})\subset\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
, and
B_{1}:=\psi_{1}^{-1}(U_{12})\subset\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
is a smooth map between (open subsets) of cornered spaces. But this follows precisely as in Lem.
4.18
, for
f=\psi^{-1}_{1}\circ\psi_{2}:B_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}B_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
, which is smooth by the manifold-with-corners structure assumption on
M
.

Since these induced charts preserve the
local
fiber-product decomposition, it follows that
T_{\mathbbm{D}}M
qualifies as a smooth fiber bundle over
M
.

Lastly, as in the case of cornered model spaces, for any smooth map
g:M\rightarrow N
of manifolds with corners we obtain a map of sets

T_{\mathbbm{D}}g\,:\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}N

by postcomposition with
g
in
\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Hence
T_{\mathbbm{D}}
obviously acts functorially at the level of sets. Dually, this action is via precomposition with the pullback
g^{*}
on the algebra of functions

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(N),\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\big)

\displaystyle\phi

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi\circ g^{*}\,,

via which
T_{\mathbbm{D}}g:T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}N
can be shown to be smooth in any two (induced) local charts for
M,N
, precisely as in Lem.
4.18
, and hence qualifies as a (globally) smooth map of manifolds with corners.
∎

We close this Appendix by showing that the very same smooth structure can be obtained directly, and equivalently, in the topos of
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
, a fact that we has been instrumental in a crucial field theoretic application (Lem.
2.29
, Thm.
3.17
). This generalizes the case of the synthetic tangent bundle of manifolds from Prop.
2.22
.

Proposition 4.20

(
Synthetic Weil bundles).

Upon embedding manifolds with corners into thickened smooth sets (Thm.
4.14
), the (non-thickened) smooth structure of the “synthetic Weil bundle”,

[\mathbbm{D},\,M],

naturally coincides with that of the traditional Weil bundle

[\mathbbm{D},\,M]\,\cong\,y(T_{\mathbbm{D}}M)\;\;\in\;\;\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Proof.

The claim is that the smooth
\mathbbm{R}^{q}
-plots of both spaces are in natural bijection. On the left-hand side, we have

[\mathbbm{D},\,M](\mathbbm{R}^{q})\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}(\mathbbm{D}\times\mathbbm{R}^{q},\,M)\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\otimes C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\big)\,,

by definition and the Yoneda Lemma. On the other hand, by the Yoneda embedding,

y(T_{\mathbbm{D}}M)(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}}(\mathbbm{R}^{q},\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}M)\,.

Let us first show the case of
M=\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}
being the model cornered space, having fixed some basis of
\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\cong W
and hence also a (global) chart
T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\cong\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}
. One way to see that the two corresponding morphism sets are in canonical bijection is to first identify (Prop.
4.13
) the latter with
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}),\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\big)
and noting that all algebra morphisms appearing are completely determined by their action on the coordinate functions. A simple counting argument then yields the result.

Alternatively and more directly by working at the point-set level, an arbitrary map of sets

\displaystyle\phi^{q}\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{q}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}

\displaystyle y_{0}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi^{q}_{y_{0}}

is smooth with respect to the canonical smooth structure of
T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,q}
(Lem.
4.17
) if and only if each component of the composition

\displaystyle\big(\phi^{q}(t^{1}),\cdots,\,\phi^{q}(t^{n+k})\big)\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{q}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{(w-1)(n+k)}

\displaystyle y_{0}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\phi^{q}_{y_{0}}\longmapsto\big(\phi^{q}_{y_{0}}(t^{1}),\cdots,\,\phi^{q}_{y_{0}}(t^{n+k})\big)

is smooth in
y_{0}\in\mathbbm{R}^{q}
. But if that’s the case, it follows also
that the value of
\phi^{q}
on any function
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,q})
is smooth in
y_{0}\in\mathbbm{R}^{q}
: Namely, expanding
f=f(t^{1},\cdots,t^{n+k})
via Hadamard’s Lemma
2.6
and applying the algebra property of
\phi^{q}
results into a (finite) product of the smooth
\{\phi^{q}(t^{i})\}_{i=1,\cdots,n+k}
in W, so that

\phi^{q}(f)\,\in\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\otimes W

for all
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k})
.
In other words,

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}}(\mathbbm{R}^{q},\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,q})\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}),\,\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\otimes C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\big)\,,

as expected.

Consider now the case of an general manifold with corners

T_{\mathbbm{D}}M\,=\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\big)\,,

so that an arbitrary map of sets

\displaystyle\phi^{q}\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{q}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}M

is smooth, by definition (Prop.
4.19
), if and only if each

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\psi_{i}\circ\phi^{q}\,:\,\mathbbm{R}^{q}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow T_{\mathbbm{D}}U_{i}\xlongrightarrow{\sim}T_{\mathbbm{D}}\mathbbm{R}^{n,q}

is smooth, for some cover of charts
\{\psi_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}\}_{i\in I}
of
M
. By the previous local model case, each of the latter maps is smooth if and only if they define algebra maps over the chart-algebras taking values in
C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\otimes W
,

T_{\mathbbm{D}}\psi_{i}\circ\phi^{q}\quad\in\quad\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{n,k}),\,C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\otimes W\big)\,.

By construction, these are compatible over overlaps in
|M|
, hence by the petit sheaf property of
C^{\infty}(M)
over
|M|
, they glue to yield a unique morphism

C^{\infty}(M)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\otimes W\,.

Thus,

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{SmthMfd}^{\mathrm{cor}}}(\mathbbm{R}^{q},\,T_{\mathbbm{D}}M)\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}^{\mathrm{op}}}\big(C^{\infty}(M),\,\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\otimes C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{q})\big)\,,

as required.
∎

The general thickened
(\mathbbm{R}^{q}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})
-plot case has been proven for Weil bundles of manifolds with boundary
[
Ko81
, Thm. 3]
[
Re07
, Prop. 2.8]
. We expect the proof therein to hold with minimal modifications, given our Prop.
4.13
and Thm.
4.14
, but we do not expand on these details it is not necessary for the field-theoretic scope of this manuscript.

4.3
Synthetic jet bundle and prolongation

In this part of the Appendix, we explain how the jet bundle itself, along with its thickened smooth structure, may be identified as a natural functorial construction within
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
. The same can be said for the jet prolongation operation of sections of jet bundles.
Towards this, we straightforwardly extend the construction of the infinitesimal neighborhoods (Defs.
2.39
,
2.44
) to the following “ infinitesimal neighborhood along
morphism
”
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
.

Definition 4.21

(
Infinitesimal neighborhood along a morphism).

Let any map
\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be any morphism of thickened smooth sets. The “
infinitesimal neighborhood along

\pi
”,
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
, is defined as the pullback

\textstyle{\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}\circ\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}\,.}

Explicitly, this is the thickened smooth set with plots

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\cong\Big\{\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\psi^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\times\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\;\big|\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}=\pi\big(\psi^{k}_{\epsilon=0}\big)\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\Big\}\,,

(108)

i.e., pairs of plots in
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
, which agree in their finite extension upon mapping the latter in
\mathcal{F}
, but are free to explore infinitesimal directions otherwise.
Denoting the left vertical map by

\pi^{\mathfrak{T}}\,:\,\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\,,

we see that its fibers in
\mathcal{F}
are precisely all plots in
\mathcal{G}
, which agree upon projecting to finite directions in
\mathcal{F}
.

Moreover, consider
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}
as the slice category over a fixed thickened smooth set
\mathcal{F}
, thought of as ‘bundles over
\mathcal{F}
’. By the universal property of the pullback, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\;:\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}

(109)

\displaystyle(\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;(\pi^{\mathfrak{T}}:\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F})

is a functor. Explicitly, if
h:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is a map of bundles over
\mathcal{F}
, there is an induced map of corresponding morphism infinitesimal neighborhoods over
\mathcal{F}
acting (naturally) on plots as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}(h)\;:\;\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)

\displaystyle\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\psi^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},h(\psi^{k}_{\epsilon})\big)\,.

Example 4.22

(
Diagonal neighborhood).

Consider the case where
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{F}
and the right vertical map is
\eta_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}
. Then the above construction defines the
(synthetic) infinitesimal neighborhood of the diagonal
(see e.g.
[
Ko16
, §4]
), or simply
diagonal neighborhood
,
\mathfrak{T}\mathcal{F}

as the pullback

\textstyle{\mathfrak{T}\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}\,.}

The name is justified since
\mathbbm{R}^{n}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of
\mathfrak{T}\mathcal{F}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\cong\Big\{\big(\phi^{k}_{1,\epsilon},\phi^{k}_{2,\epsilon}\big)\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\times\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\;\big|\;\phi^{k}_{1,\epsilon=0}=\phi^{k}_{2,\epsilon=0}\,\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\Big\}\,,

that is, pairs of plots of
\mathcal{F}
that agree on their purely finite directions, but are free to differ in their infinitesimal extensions.

Example 4.23

(
Infinitesimal neighborhoods around submanifolds).

Choosing
\mathcal{F}=M
a smooth manifold and the vertical map to be
\Sigma\hookrightarrow M
recovers precisely the definitions of infinitesimal neighborhoods of points and submanifolds from Def.
2.39
and Def.
2.44
, respectively.

Similarly, it follows that the set of
\infty
-jets of sections of
\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
of Def.
2.42
may be equivalently written as

\displaystyle J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(*)=\underset{p\in\mathcal{F}(*)}{\bigcup}\!\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}/\big(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}(*)\,,\,\mathcal{G}\big)\,.

The above formula is suggestive of a way to define the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of a would-be thickened smooth set
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
. Indeed, for any plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
,i.e., equivalently by Yoneda lemma a morphism
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon{*}}\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
we may consider

\displaystyle\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\;:

\displaystyle=\;\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon{*}}:y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\big)\,,

(110)

the
infinitesimal neighborhood around the
\mathbbm{R}^{n}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
. Sections over all of these are hence deserving to be considered as
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of the corresponding the
\infty
-jet bundle.

Definition 4.24

(
Synthetic jet bundle).

Let
\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a map of thickened smooth sets. Define the
synthetic
\infty
-jet bundle

J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
as the assignment

\displaystyle J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,:=\underset{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})}{\bigcup}\!\!\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\big(\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\,,\,\mathcal{G}\big)\,,

(111)

for any probe
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{ThCartSp}
.

The definition is in line with intuition. The points of
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
are given by sections over infinitesimal neighborhoods of points in the base, while similarly
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
are given by sections over infinitesimal neighborhoods around
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots in the base. For this to define a thickened smooth set, it remains to extend the assignment functorially to maps between probe spaces. This is canonical.

Lemma 4.25

(
Synthetic jet bundle is a thickened smooth set).

The assignment of the synthetic
\infty
-jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
defines canonically a functor
\mathrm{ThCartSp}^{op}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}
, which furthermore satisfies the sheaf condition.

Proof.

Let
f:\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
be a map of thickened Cartesian spaces, denote by
f_{*}:y(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
its image under the Yoneda embedding and by
f^{*}:\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})
the corresponding map of plots of any formal smooth set
\mathcal{F}
. There is a map

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}\big(y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}),\mathcal{F})\big)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathrm{Hom}\big(y(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}),\mathcal{F}\big)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}_{\epsilon{*}}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon{*}}\circ f_{*}=\big(f^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)_{*}

for any
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon{*}}
corresponding to a plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
, by the Yoneda Lemma.
Furthermore, for each pair of a plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
and a map of Cartesian spaces
f
, there exists canonical a smooth map

\displaystyle f_{!}:\mathbbm{D}_{f^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\big(\phi^{m}_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}},\psi^{m}_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\phi^{m}_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}},f_{*}(\psi^{m}_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}})\big)

for each
\mathbbm{R}^{m}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime\prime}
-plot of
\mathbbm{D}_{f^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}
, identified with an element of
\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{m}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime\prime})\times\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbbm{R}^{m}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime\prime},\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})
by (
108
). It follows that the image is indeed a plot of
D_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}
, since by definition
\phi^{m}_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}=0}=(\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\circ f)\circ\psi^{m}_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}=0}
. Abstractly, the map
f_{!}
is uniquely determined by the universal property of the pullback
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}
, since the two pullbacks paste as

\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{f^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}}
\textstyle{y(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}\circ\phi^{k}_{\epsilon{*}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{F}}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{J}\mathcal{F}\,.}

With the map
f_{!}
at hand, we may precompose any section
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}
over
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},M}
as

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{f^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}}

yielding a section
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\circ f_{!}
over
\mathbbm{D}_{f^{*}\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}
. This construction works for every
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
, and so yields a map

\displaystyle(-)\circ f_{!}\;\;:\;\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{G})\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\,.

(112)

Finally, by construction
(g\circ f)_{!}=g_{!}\circ f_{!}
and so
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
defines a functor
\mathrm{ThCartSp}^{op}\rightarrow\mathrm{Set}
. Lastly, it is not hard to check the sheaf condition explicitly.
∎

The
\infty
-jet bundle comes equipped with a canonical (smooth) projection

\displaystyle\pi^{\infty}\quad:\quad J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\qquad

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\;\mathcal{F}

(113)

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\xrightarrow{\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}\Big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\;\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}

sending any section
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}
over the infinitesimal neighborhood around a plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
to the plot itself. It follows that for any fixed thickened
\mathcal{F}
smooth set, we get a functor of bundles over
\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\;:\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}

(114)

\displaystyle\big(\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\big)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\pi^{\infty}:J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\big)\,,

as for any bundle map
h:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
over
\mathcal{F}
, there is an induced map of
\infty
-jet bundles over
\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(h)\;:\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\,

(115)

\displaystyle\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;h\circ\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\,,

acting by post-composition on the corresponding sections

\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}
over infinitesimal nbds of
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots. Naturality under pullbacks of plots
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\circ f_{!}
follows as in Lemma
4.25
, by associativity of composition,

J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}h\big(\sigma_{\epsilon}^{k}\circ f_{!}\big)=h\circ\big(\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\circ f_{!}\big)=\big(h\circ\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)\circ f_{!}=J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(h(\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon})\big)\circ f_{!}\;.

Remark 4.26

(
Applicability to non-concrete smooth sets).

The construction of the synthetic
\infty
-jet bundle is quite general. Beyond bundles of smooth manifolds, it applies to maps between arbitrary thickened smooth sets, even if they do not actually have any underlying points (cf. Def.
4.4
), i.e. is not “concrete”. Even in such a situation, the infinite jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}
exists, and will be non-concrete itself, but potentially with non-trivial
\mathbbm{R}^{k}
-plots and their thickened versions for
k\neq 0
.

Nevertheless, this more general construction still satisfies many properties of the traditional infinite jet bundle. For example, we have the following property.

Example 4.27

(
Jet bundle of identity bundle).

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a thickened smooth set and consider
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
as a bundle over itself. There is a canonical isomorphism

J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{F}\;\cong\;\mathcal{F}\,.

Indeed, to see this
let
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
be a plot, a section
\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}
of
\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
over the infinitesimal disk
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}
around the plot is the same as a lift

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\;\;}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\,.}

Commutativity of the diagram gives
\mathrm{id}\circ\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}=\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon}=\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}
, and so fixes the
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{F}
to be the set of canonical inclusions of infinitesimal disks around
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
plots of
\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})=\underset{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})}{\bigcup}\!\!\!\Big\{\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}}}\mathcal{F}\Big\}\,.

Define the canonical map on plots to be the obvious one

\displaystyle g\;\;:\quad J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\;\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\;}}\mathcal{F}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\,,

which is bijective since, by construction of
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}
in (
110
), there exists a unique such canonical inclusion
\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}
for each plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
