. What remains to be shown is that the assignment is functional under maps of probes, but this follows by similar arguments.

Example 4.28

(
Synthetic jet bundle of trivial bundle).

Consider the ‘trivial bundle’
\mathrm{pr}_{2}:\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
, then an
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plot of
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F})
is a map
\big(\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon,1},\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon,2}\big):\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F}
that satisfies the section condition. This fixes the form of the map to be
\big(\sigma^{k}_{\epsilon,1},\,\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\big):\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F}
. Equivalently, we have

J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F})(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\cong\underset{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})}{\bigcup}\!\!\!\big\{\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}\big\}\,.

Following the manifold intuition, this is to be thought of as the space of ‘jets of maps’
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
. Notice, there might be many maps
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
for each plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}
of
\mathcal{F}
and so
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F})
is not isomorphic to
\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F}
, unless
\mathcal{H}=*
by the previous example. Nevertheless, there is still a canonical ‘inclusion’ map

\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F}\,\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\,J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F})

that sends a plot
\big(\psi^{k}_{\epsilon},\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)
to the section

\big(\psi^{k}_{\epsilon*}\circ\pi_{2}\,,\,\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\big)\;:\;\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\,\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\,\mathcal{H}\times\mathcal{F}

over
\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\xrightarrow{\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}}\mathcal{F}
, where
\pi_{2}:\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\rightarrow y(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
denotes the canonical map of the pullback definition (
110
)  .
Note that for manifolds
U\times M\rightarrow M
, the map corresponds to
(u,p)\mapstochar\rightarrow j^{\infty}_{u}(\mathrm{const}_{p})
.

By construction, the infinitesimal neighborhood bundle functor
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
and the
\infty
-jet bundle
J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}
functor
are closely related, in the precise sense of forming an adjunction. An abstract proof of this relying on differential cohesion may be found in
[
KS17
]
. Here, we provide an equivalent proof that relies on elementary but more explicit facts.

Proposition 4.29

(
Infinitesimal neighborhood bundle / Jet bundle adjunction).

For each
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
,
the infinitesimal neighborhood bundle functor and the
\infty
-jet bundle functor form an adjunction

\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\;\;\dashv\;\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\quad:\quad\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}\,.

Proof.

The claim is that there exists a bijection

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\big(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{H},\,\mathcal{G}\big)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\big(\mathcal{H},\,J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\big)

which is natural in both
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{G}
, with the fixed maps to
\mathcal{F}
left implicit. Considering first the case where

\mathcal{G}=\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
is a representable object equipped with a plot
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}:\mathbbm{R}^{n}\times\mathbbm{D}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
,
then

\displaystyle\underset{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})}{\bigcup}\!\!\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\Big(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)\,,\,\mathcal{G}\Big)

\displaystyle\;=:\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle\;\cong\;\underset{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})}{\bigcup}\!\!\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\Big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D},\,J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\Big),

where the first line is by definition, while the second is by Yoneda lemma. In particular, this implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\Big(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)\,,\,\mathcal{G}\Big)\;\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\Big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D},\,J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\Big)

naturally in
\mathcal{G}
and in the representable space
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
, and so the adjunction holds when the source is an arbitrary representable.

But any source sheaf
\mathcal{H}
may be written as the colimit of representables,
\mathcal{H}\cong\underset{i\in I}{\mathrm{colim}}\,(\mathbbm{R}^{k_{i}}\times\mathbbm{D}_{i})
and since, by stability of colimits in sheaf topoi,
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}
preserves colimits, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{H},\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\;\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\bigg(\underset{i\in I}{\mathrm{colim}}\,\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k_{i}}\times\mathbbm{D}_{i})\,,\,\mathcal{G}\bigg)

\displaystyle\cong\;\underset{i\in I}{\lim}\,\mathrm{Hom}_{{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\big(\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathbbm{R}^{k_{i}}\times\mathbbm{D}_{i})\,,\,\mathcal{G}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\;\underset{i\in I}{\lim}\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k_{i}}\times\mathbbm{D}_{i}\,,\,J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}_{/\mathcal{F}}}\big(\mathcal{H},J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G})\,.

∎

The following intuitively expected result is originally due to
[
KS17
]
, which shows that the traditional notion of an infinite jet bundle together with its (thickened) smooth structure coincides with the internal synthetic definition.

Theorem 4.30

(
Synthetic jet bundle of fiber bundle).

For any fiber bundle
F\rightarrow M
of finite-dimensional manifolds, the synthetic jet bundle construction (Def.
4.24
) recovers precisely the corresponding infinite jet bundle limit
J^{\infty}_{M}F
formed in thickened smooth sets (Def.
2.33
), i.e.,

J^{\infty}_{y(M)}y(F)\,\cong\,J^{\infty}_{M}F\,,

where
J^{\infty}_{M}F=\lim_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}y\big(J^{k}_{M}F\big)
.

Proof.

(Sketch) The points of both smooth sets coincide, since by construction

J^{\infty}_{y(M)}y(F)(*)\;:=\;\underset{p\in M}{\bigcup}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}/y(M)}\big(\mathbbm{D}_{p,M}\,,\,y(F)\big)\;\cong\;\underset{p\in M}{\bigcup}J^{\infty}_{p}F=:y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(*)\,,

due to Lem.
2.37
and Lem.
2.40
. The rest of the proof can be found in
[
KS17
]
, following similarly as an application of the above adjunction and the local triviality of finite-dimensional manifolds.
∎

In view of the former theorem, Lem.
4.27
applied to the case of
\mathrm{id}:M\rightarrow M
for a finite-dimensional manifold translates to the statement that the identity map has vanishing traditional jets. Similarly, Ex.
4.28
applied to the case of
F\times M\rightarrow M
recovers the (smooth) set of infinity jets of maps
M\rightarrow F
. Furthermore, the theorem reassures that the synthetic infinite bundle is the natural generalization of the usual jet bundle of a finite-dimensional fiber bundle, in the vast realm of maps between (thickened) smooth spaces, potentially infinite-dimensional, with no local triviality and even non-concrete spaces. Indeed, the jet prolongation of sections may defined internaly and naturally in this context too.

Definition 4.31

(
Synthetic infinite jet prolongation).

Let
\pi:\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a map of smooth sets and
\pi^{\infty}:J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
its infinite jet bundle. The
infinite jet prolongation
is the map (of sets)

j^{\infty}\;:\;\Gamma_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{G})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma_{\mathcal{F}}(J^{\infty}\mathcal{G})

defined by the composition

\displaystyle(\sigma:\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G})\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\Big(\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\sigma)}J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}\Big)\,,

where the first identification on the right is given in Lem.
4.27
and the second map is the application of the infinity jet functor (
115
) on the smooth map
\sigma
.

Explicitly, in terms of plots the section
j^{\infty}\sigma
is the assignment that fits in the diagram

\textstyle{J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\,,}

naturally in
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
, given by

\displaystyle j^{\infty}\sigma\;:\;\mathcal{F}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\mathcal{G}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\sigma\circ\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}:\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},\mathcal{F}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\big)\,.

The synthetic infinite jet prolongation naturally extends to a smooth map between the corresponding smooth sets of sections of the corresponding bundles,

\displaystyle j^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\;:\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{G})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{F}}(J^{\infty}\mathcal{G})\,,

(116)

where the (thickened) smooth set structure of the spaces of sections is exactly as in the finite-dimensional case
(
2.3
), i.e.,

\mathbold{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{G})\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)=\Big\{\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\,:\,y\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\;\;\big|\;\;\pi\circ\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}=\mathrm{pr}_{2}\Big\}\,,

with
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}
plots of smooth sections defined as
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}
-parametrized sections of
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
, and similarly for
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{F}}(J^{\infty}\mathcal{G})
. Indeed,

j^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\;:\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{G})\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{F}}(J^{\infty}\mathcal{G})\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)

is defined by the composition

\displaystyle\Big(\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\,:\,y\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\Big)\;\;\longmapsto\;\;\bigg(y\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\times\mathcal{F}\hookrightarrow J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}\big(y(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\times\mathcal{F}\big)\xrightarrow{\;J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}})\;}J^{\infty}_{\mathcal{F}}(\mathcal{G})\!\bigg)

with the first inclusion on the right being that of Ex.
4.28
. Explicitly, the smooth map on the right is given on plots by

\big(\psi^{k}_{\epsilon},\,\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\big)\;\longmapsto\;\Big(\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\circ\big(\psi^{k}_{\epsilon*}\circ\pi_{2}\,,\,\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\big)\;:\,\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}\rightarrow y\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\times\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\Big)\,.

Lemma 4.32

(
Synthetic jet prolongation for fiber bundles).

Let
\pi:F\rightarrow M
be a fiber bundle of finite-dimensional manifolds. The synthetic prolongation

j^{\infty}_{y(M)}\;:\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(y(F)\big)\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(J^{\infty}y(F)\big)\cong\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(y(J^{\infty}F)\big)

coincides with the traditional (smooth) jet prolongation

j^{\infty}_{M}\;:\;\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)

from Lem.
3.8
, under the natural identifications of the corresponding smooth sets. That is, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(y(F)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}_{y(M)}}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(J^{\infty}y(F)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(y(J^{\infty}F)\big)}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\infty}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

commutes, where the (canonical) vertical isomorphisms are those of Yoneda Lemma.

Proof.

Denote by
\sigma_{*}
the Yoneda embedding of a section
\sigma:M\rightarrow F
. The synthetic jet prolongation gives a section
j^{\infty}\sigma_{*}:y(M)\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{y(M)}\big(y(F)\big)
which acts on points by

\displaystyle y(M)(*)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{\infty}_{y(M)}y(F)(*)\cong y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(*)

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\sigma_{*}\circ\iota_{p}:\mathbbm{D}_{p,y(M)}\rightarrow F\big)\,,

where the isomorphism on the right-hand side is by the previous theorem. By Lem.
2.37
and Lem.
2.40
, this corresponds to
p\mapstochar\rightarrow j^{\infty}_{p}\sigma\in J^{\infty}_{M}(F)
, i.e., the traditional prolongation
j^{\infty}_{M}\sigma:M\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{M}(F)
. Analogously,
j^{\infty}\sigma_{*}
acts on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots by

\displaystyle y(M)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{\infty}_{y(M)}y(F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})\cong y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\big)

\displaystyle\phi^{k}_{\epsilon*}

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\big(\sigma_{*}\circ\iota_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}}:\mathbbm{D}_{\phi^{k}_{\epsilon},y(M)}\rightarrow F\big)\,.

Arguing similarly to Lem.
2.37
and Lem.
2.40
, this corresponds to
\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\mapstochar\rightarrow j^{\infty}_{M}\sigma\circ\phi^{k}_{\epsilon}\in y(J^{\infty}_{M}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D})
. Thus overall, the synthetic prolongation
j^{\infty}\sigma_{*}
of a smooth section
\sigma:M\rightarrow F
coincides with (the Yoneda embedding of) the traditional prolonged smooth section
j^{\infty}_{M}\sigma
.

The above shows that the two jet prolongation maps agree on sections of the bundle, i.e., points of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)\cong\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(y(F)\big)
. It remains to show they agree on
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}
-parametrized sections of the bundle, i.e.
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}
-plots of
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)
. As before, for
\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})
denote by
\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}*}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{y(M)}\big(y(F)\big)(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})
the corresponding plot. Then its synthetic jet prolongation is a smooth map
j^{\infty}\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}*}:y(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\times y(M)\rightarrow J^{\infty}_{y(M)}\big(y(F)\big)
which acts on points by

\displaystyle y\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\big)\times y(M)(*)

\displaystyle\;\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;J^{\infty}_{y(M)}y(F)(*)\cong y\big(J^{\infty}_{M}F\big)(*)

\displaystyle(x,p)

\displaystyle\;\longmapsto\;\Big(\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}*}\circ(x\circ\pi_{2},\iota_{p}):\mathbbm{D}_{p,y(M)}\rightarrow\big(y(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})\times y(M)\big)\rightarrow F\Big)\,,

In a similar vein as before, it can be checked that this corresponds to the (vertical) traditional jet prolongation
j^{\infty}_{M}\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}\in\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(J^{\infty}F)(\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime})
, when acting on the underlying points of
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\times M
. Modulo explicit complicated formulas, it follows that
j^{\infty}\sigma^{k^{\prime}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}*}
corresponds to the traditional jet prolongation acting on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
-plots of
\mathbbm{R}^{k^{\prime}}\times\mathbbm{D}^{\prime}\times M
.
∎

4.4
The full classifying nature of the de Rham moduli space

In this manuscript we have defined the moduli space of de Rham forms (Ex.
2.17
) over the site of thickened Cartesian spaces, by assigning an algebraic version of forms as its plots along infinitesimal probes. By the fully faithful embedding of manifolds into thickened smooth sets (Ex.
2.14
), the fact that
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthMfd})\cong\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
and the Yoneda Lemma, this does classify forms
on smooth manifolds
in the traditional sense

\Omega^{1}(M):=\mathrm{Hom}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}_{\mathrm{SmthMfd}}(TM,\mathbbm{R})\;\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}}(M,\mathbold{\Omega}^{1})\,,

and similarly for
n
-forms.

However, the full classifying nature of
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}
as originally envisioned in the setting of synthetic differential geometry
[
La80
]
, where it should modulate / classify forms defined as maps out of arbitrary synthetic tangent bundles
T\mathcal{F}=[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\,\mathcal{F}]
, requires a slightly different extension in the thickened setting rather than the “Kähler” extension of Ex.
2.17
. That is, instead of defining its infinitesimal plots by generalizing the algebraic incarnation of forms on the tangent bundle, one defines its plots via the – maps out of the (synthetic) tangent bundle – picture. For brevity, and for reasons of further generality, in this Appendix we shall denote
\mathcal{E}:=\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
and any thickened Cartesian probe as
e\in E:=\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
.

Definition 4.33

(
Fully classifying moduli space of de Rham forms).

The fully classifying
thickened moduli space of de Rham
1
-forms

\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}
is defined as the sheaf
\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
with
e
-plots given by

\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}(e)\,:=\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}\big(Te,\,\mathbbm{R}\big)\,.

Remark 4.34

(
Original form moduli definitions).

The original axiomatic definition (cf.
[
La80
, §3]
[
Ko06
, §I.20]
) identifies the above object instead as a certain equalizer, which can be shown to be equivalent to the above when expressed plot-wise in our setting. On the other hand, the original source
[
DK84
, §8]
, which firstly identifies the classifying space in an explicit sheaf topos, works over a larger category of probes whose (different) definition may still be shown to coincide with an extension of the above in such a larger sheaf topos (Cor.
4.42
).

Of course, this version of the moduli space still classifies differential forms on ordinary manifolds for exactly the same reason, i.e., by the Yoneda Lemma. Moreover, as we shall see, if
Te
were representable in
\mathcal{E}
(which is not the case for thickened smooth sets), then this definition would coincide with that of Ex.
2.17
(Cor.
4.42
). Otherwise, as in our sheaf topos, the relation between the two is that the original
\mathbold{\Omega}
sits as a subspace inside the latter
\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}
(Lem.
4.38
). The reason for the latter’s more “internal” definition will become clear from the following discussion.

The amazing right adjoint.

Recall, by the properties of the internal hom functor, the synthetic tangent functor
T(-):=[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),-]:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
enjoys a left adjoint (
30
),

\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times-\big)\quad\dashv\quad T(-)\,,

a property that has proven to be extremely useful in thickened smooth sets (cf. Lem.
2.27
, Prop.
2.28
, Lem.
2.34
, Cor.
3.20
).
The fact that the synthetic tangent functor should also have a right adjoint was first pointed out in the context of synthetic differential geometry by
[
La80
]
.
38
38

38

Since then, an object
K\in C
of a Cartesian closed category
C
whose mapping space functor
[K,-]
has a right adjoint is called “tiny” or “atomic”.

If such a right adjoint exists, then
[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),-]
must preserve colimits, which is indeed the case (see
[
MR91
, Appendix 4]
). It turns out that for any sheaf topos, such as
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
and
\mathcal{E}
, preserving colimits is also sufficient for the existence of a right adjoint, denoted by
(-)_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
,

39
39

39

As proven in
[
MR91
]
,
any
infinitesimal point
\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{ThCartSp}\hookrightarrow E
in our site has the property that
[\mathbbm{D},-]
preserves colimits, and hence has also a right adjoint
(-)_{\mathbbm{D}}
given by the same formula.

\big(\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)\times-\big)\quad\dashv\quad T(-)\quad\dashv\quad(-)_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\,.

In other words, there is a canonical bijection

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathcal{G})\;\;\cong\;\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\big)

(117)

for any two smooth spaces
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
, naturally in both entries. Explicitly for any
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{E}
, the space
\mathcal{G}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}
is defined via
40
40

40

The formula
defines a
presheaf

\mathcal{G}_{U}
for any
U\in E
. It is the colimit-preserving property of
[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),-]
that guarantees the sheaf condition.

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\,:\,\mathrm{E}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathrm{Set}

\displaystyle e

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\,e\big]\,,\,\mathcal{G}\big)\,,

and so by definition of the synthetic tangent bundle, the
e
-plots consist of all smooth maps
Te\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
from the tangent bundle of
e
(
120
). Of particular importance for us is
the value of this right adjoint on the real line,

\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\;\;\in\;\;\mathcal{E}\,,

whose
e
-plots are identified of
all real-valued smooth maps
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te,\mathbbm{R})
, thus potentially
non-linear
in the fibers. It follows by the adjoint bijection (
117
) that this thickened space
classifies real valued maps out of the synthetic tangent bundle

\mathcal{F}
of any space sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{E}
, in that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R})\;\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\big)\,.

(118)

With the fully classifying moduli space

\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{SmthSet}
defined as in Def.
4.33
, it is immediately seen to be a subobject of
\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}
, as its
e
-plots are simply the subset of
linear
(smooth) maps
Te\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
in
\mathcal{E}
.

Corollary 4.35

(
Form moduli as linear subobject).

The moduli space of 1-forms
\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}
is canonically identified with a (thickened smooth) subspace of
\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\quad\in\;\;\mathcal{E}\,.

Our approach to the definition of the moduli space now allows for a direct
41
41

41

At least, somewhat more direct than the original source

[
DK84
]
.
extraction of its classifying property. Indeed, given the above embedding and the fact that
\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}
classifies
all real-valued
maps out of synthetic tangent bundles
T\mathcal{F}
(cf. (
118
)), the classifying nature of the moduli space of 1-forms
\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\in\mathcal{E}
becomes apparent: It classifies is a subset of
smooth maps
T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
out of the synthetic tangent bundle, which in the case of (an infinitesimally linear)
\mathcal{F}
with a fiber-wise
\mathbbm{R}
-linear structure coincides with
those smooth maps that are furthermore linear in the fibers.

Proposition 4.36

(
Classifying nature of form moduli).

(i)

For any generalized thickened smooth space
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{E}
, there is a canonical injection

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R}\big)\,.

(ii)

Furthermore, for any (infinitesimally linear)
\mathcal{F}
whose tangent bundle
T\mathcal{F}
has a fiber-wise
\mathbbm{R}
-linear structure, the image of this inclusion corresponds to fiber-wise linear maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)\;\cong\;\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}\big(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R}\big)\,.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be any thickened smooth space. Postcomposing any smooth map
\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}
with the monomorphism of Cor.
4.35
yields the
injection

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\big)\,.

Under the canonical bijection of the adjoint relation (
117
), this yields

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}\big)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R})\,,

proving the first statement.

Now notice that when
\mathcal{F}
is considered as a colimit on representable probes,
\mathcal{F}\cong\mathrm{colim}_{i}(e^{i})
, then we also have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathrm{colim}_{i}(e^{i}),\,\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\lim_{i,\mathrm{Set}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(e^{i},\,\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\lim_{i,\mathrm{Set}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}\big(Te^{i},\,\mathbbm{R}\big)\,,

where we used that Hom functors commute with (co)limits in the first entry, and then Def.
4.33
along with the Yoneda Lemma.
Next, consider the case where
T\mathcal{F}
has a fiber-wise linear structure. Since fiber-wise linearity is a functorial property, i.e., a composition of fiber-wise linear maps is also such, it follows that the image of the inclusion

\displaystyle\lim_{i,\mathrm{Set}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}(Te^{i},\,\mathbbm{R})\;\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\;\lim_{i,\mathrm{Set}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te^{i},\,\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{colim}_{i}(Te^{i}),\,\mathbbm{R})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R})

is the set of the fiber-wise linear maps
T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
, due to the universal cocone property of the colimit.
That is,

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\big)\,\cong\,\lim_{i,\mathrm{Set}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}\big(Te^{i},\,\mathbbm{R}\big)\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}(T\mathcal{F},\,\mathbbm{R})\,\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(T\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{R})\,.

The detailed justification of the latter intuition
42
42

42

This seems to be only implicit in the original proof from
[
DK84
, Thm. 8.2]
.
is the fact that the colimit
\mathrm{colim}_{i}(Te^{i})
may be equivalently computed in the slice category,
\mathrm{FibLin}_{\mathcal{F}}
, of fiber-wise linear “bundles” over
\mathcal{F}
. Indeed, this follows since corresponding pushforward maps
\{Te^{i}\rightarrow T\mathcal{F}\}_{i\in I}
of the diagram
\{e^{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\}_{i\in I}
are all fiber-wise linear and they form a covering of
T\mathcal{F}
(in the ambient topos, i.e., a jointly epic family), by virtue of
\{e^{i}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\}_{i\in I}
being such a covering of
\mathcal{F}
and
T=[-\,,\,\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)]
preserving jointly epic families (since it preserves colimits, being a left adjoint). The latter implies precisely that
T\mathcal{F}
satisfies the corresponding universal cocone property in
\mathrm{FibLin}_{\mathcal{F}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{F}}
, namely given a compatible family of fiber-wise linear maps
f_{i}:Te^{i}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
, there exists a unique fiber-wise linear
f:T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
such that
f_{i}=f\circ Tp_{i}
.
43
43

43

This argument is the generalization, in some (well-adapted) topos, of the following simple fact from (Cartesian) vector spaces: Consider a colimit of vector spaces
p_{i}:\mathbbm{R}^{m_{i}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}^{m}
and some
smooth
map
f:\mathbbm{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
with the property that each
f\circ p_{i}
is linear. Then
f
is also necessarily linear, since each
f|_{\mathrm{Im}(p_{i})}
is linear and
\mathop{\text{\smash{\raisebox{-9.03763pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1]{$\prod$}}}\vphantom{$\prod$}}}\mathrm{Im}(p_{i})
covers
\mathbbm{R}^{m}
, in the set theoretic sense.

∎

Remark 4.37

(
Vector-valued form classifying spaces).

The above results generalize straightforwardly to the case of maps
T\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
, where
\mathcal{V}
has a
\mathbbm{R}
-module structure, for instance if
\mathcal{V}=y(V)
is the embedding of a
finite-dimensional vector space
V\in\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbbm{R}}^{\mathrm{f.d.}}
(viewed as a smooth manifold), or even a thickened smooth set of sections
\mathbold{\Gamma}_{M}(V)
of vector bundle
V\rightarrow M
. In such a situation, working as above, one may
identify a sub-object
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(-,\mathcal{V})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1)}
which classifies fiber-wise linear maps into
\mathcal{V}
.

Let us now make explicit the relation of the above to the algebraic definition of the moduli space
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}
from Ex.
4.4
.

Lemma 4.38

(
Relation of the two moduli spaces).

The algebraic moduli space is naturally a subspace of the fully classifying moduli space

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\,.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te,\mathbbm{R})
is the set of natural transformations between the sheaves
Te:=[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),\,e]
and
\mathbbm{R}
over
E
. By Ex.
2.26
, we have that
Te(e^{\prime}):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(\mathcal{O}(Te,\,\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})\big)
, while by the Yoneda embedding
y(\mathbbm{R})(e^{\prime})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{R}),\,\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})\big)

\cong\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})
.
Thus,

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,\cong\,\Big\{\big\{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(\mathcal{O}(Te),\,\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})\big)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})\big\}_{\mathrm{nat.}\,\mathrm{in}\,e^{\prime}\in E}\Big\}\,,

where the latter set ranges over families of maps which are natural under pullback along probes
e^{\prime}\in E
.

Now, any element
v\in\mathcal{O}(Te)
defines a family of (natural) maps on the right-hand side via its evaluation on algebra maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{v}\,:\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{CAlg}}\big(\mathcal{O}(Te),\,\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\xlongrightarrow{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}\mathcal{O}(e^{\prime})

\displaystyle\phi_{e^{\prime}}\quad

\displaystyle\longmapsto\quad\phi_{e^{\prime}}(v)\,,

which yields an inclusion

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(Te)\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,,

(119)

even in the current situation where
Te
is not representable in
\mathcal{E}
.

Moreover, in analogy with the canonical embedding

\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}(e)\,:=\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}\big(Te,\,\mathbbm{R}\big){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}\big(Te,\,\mathbbm{R}\big)

of the plots of the fully classifying moduli space into the set of
internal
real-valued maps on
TE
, there is a canonical embedding of the plots of the algebraic moduli space into the function algebra of
Te
,

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e){\mathrel{\rightarrow}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt}}\mathcal{O}(Te)

given in via any quotient representative
44
44

44

Note that the inclusion is independent of the quotient representative when both sides are pulled back to
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)
and
\mathcal{O}(Te)
.
(
14
) as

f_{i}(x)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{i}\longmapsto f_{i}(x)\cdot y^{i}\,.

The latter’s image under (
119
) can be checked to yield (internal) fiber-wise linear maps.

Summarizing the above observations, we have the following commuting diagram of inclusions

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}(Te)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}(e)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,,}

natural in e, which completes the proof.
∎

For the purposes of local field theory, the above lemma may be interpreted as the consistency of
both

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}
and
\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}
serving as classifying spaces for
local
differential forms on the product
\mathcal{F}\times M
(i.e., a field space with the spacetime the fields are defined over). Namely, by Prop.
4.36
the latter classifies
all
differential forms, i.e. fiber-wise linear maps out of
T(CF\times M)
, and hence
in particular
those of local nature (factoring through
T(J^{\infty}_{M}F)
). But by our earlier results (Rem.
3.15
), the algebraic moduli space
\mathbold{\Omega^{1}}
also classifies such local forms. By Lem.
4.38
, it must be any such two modulating maps,
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{c}
and
\mathcal{P}_{c}
, classifying the
same
local form
\mathcal{P}\in\Omega^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathcal{F}\times M)
are the “same”, in that they commute under the canonical embedding of the classifying spaces

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{c}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{c}}
\textstyle{\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\,.}

Remark 4.39

(
Differential n-form classifying spaces).

The prior discussion generalizes essentially verbatim for the case of the classifying space(s) of differential n-forms on infinitesimally linear spaces (Rem.
2.25
). In this case, the appropriate “amazing right adjoint” is instead
(-)_{\mathbbm{D}^{n}(1)}:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
,

\big(\mathbbm{D}^{n}(1)\times-\big)\quad\dashv\quad(-)^{\mathbbm{D}^{n}(1)}\quad\dashv\quad(-)_{\mathbbm{D}^{n}(1)}\,,

since for infinitesimally linear spaces
T\mathcal{F}^{\times^{n}_{\mathcal{F}}}\cong\big[\mathbbm{D}^{n}(1),\,\mathcal{F}\big]
. The above results then generalize accordingly to yield the sequence of subspace identifications

\mathbold{\Omega}^{n}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{n}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}_{\mathbbm{D}^{n}(1)}\,,

where
\mathbold{\Omega}^{n}
is the (algebraic) moduli space (Ex.
2.17
) and
\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{n}
is the fully classifying space with
e
-plots given by alternating maps
(Te)^{\times^{n}_{e}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{R}
in
\mathcal{E}
(cf. Def.
4.33
).

The case of tangent bundles being representable.

As a final aside, we note that it is possible to make the two different definitions of moduli spaces necessarily coincide, if one is willing to work over a larger site of thickened probes. We briefly show how this works here, but we note that due to the above factorization, this is not strictly necessary for the purposes of local field theory.
As we shall see, it is sufficient to consider instead some larger site
45
45

45

Supplied with some coverage, whose explicit form is not relevant for what follows.

E
of smooth loci (duals of finitely generated
C^{\infty}
-algebras)

\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow E\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{f.g.}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}\,,

which is
closed under the formation of synthetic tangent bundles
. More precisely, this means that in the category of sheaves

\mathcal{E}\,:=\,\mathrm{Sh}(E)

the synthetic tangent bundle of any object
e\in E
is representable

\big[\mathbbm{D}^{1}(1),y(e)\big]\,\cong\,y(Te)

where

Te\in E
is the formal dual of some function algebra
\mathcal{O}(Te)\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{f.g.}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}
. For instance, this property is indeed implicit in the original source of
[
DK84
]
on the classifying space of differential forms, where the classifying space is formalized explicitly within a sheaf topos for the first time.

In particular, this site should include all tangent bundles to thickened Cartesian spaces (Ex.
2.26
) defined by the function algebras
\mathcal{O}\big(T(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l))\big)\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{f.g.}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}

T\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\,\in\,E,

and similarly for all other objects in the site. More generally, by
[
MR91
, Prop. 1.11]
, this translates to the following condition on the site
E
:

e\,\in\,E\quad\implies\quad Te\,\in\,E\,,

where for any

e
being the formal dual of

\mathcal{O}(e)\,\cong\,C^{\infty}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\big)/I\,,

the corresponding “tangent bundle” is defined as the dual of

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(Te)\,:=\,C^{\infty}\Big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x}\times\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{y}\Big)\Big/\bigg(I(x),\,\bigg\{\sum_{i}y_{i}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^{i}}\,|\,f\in I\bigg\}\bigg)\,.

(120)

Examples of such sites are for instance the whole of
C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{f.g.}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, or the smaller sites
\mathbb{G}
and
\mathbb{F}
from
[
MR91
]
, whose details we do not need to recall for our considerations below
46
46

46

These sites are in fact closed under arbitrary infinitesimal exponentiations (
[
MR91
, Cor. 1.16]
).
. Of course, the “minimal” among such sites towards our goal here, extending
\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
inside
C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{f.g.}}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}^{\mathrm{op}}
, is given by including all (iterated) tangent bundles of thickened Cartesian spaces
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
, namely the formal duals of

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\Big(T\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\!\Big)

\displaystyle\cong C^{\infty}\Big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{x_{0}}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{x_{1}}\Big)\Big/\Big((x_{0})^{l+1},\,x_{1}\cdot(x_{0})^{l}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\Big(T^{2}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}^{m}(l)\big)\!\Big)

\displaystyle\cong C^{\infty}\Big(\big(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\big)^{\times 4}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{x_{0}}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{x_{1}}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{x_{2}}\times\mathbbm{R}^{m}_{x_{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle\phantom{AAAAAAAAAAAAA}\Big/\Big((x_{0})^{l+1},\,x_{1}\cdot(x_{0})^{l},\,x_{2}\cdot(x_{0})^{l},\,x_{3}\cdot(x_{0})^{l},\,x_{2}\cdot x_{1}\cdot(x_{l})^{l-1},\,x_{3}\cdot x_{1}\cdot(x_{l})^{l-1}\Big),

and so on for any
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}\in\mathrm{Th}\mathrm{CartSp}
.

The first task, then, is to provide an appropriate working definition of the
algebraic
classifying space as a sheaf over this more general category of thickened probe-spaces, extending that of Ex.
2.17
. Since these are duals of
finitely-generated

C^{\infty}
-algebras, one can define this directly as follows (cf.
[
Jo19
, Prop. 5.6]
).

Definition 4.40

(
Thickened moduli space of de Rham forms).

(i)

The
thickened moduli space of de Rham
1
-forms

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}
is defined as the sheaf
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}\in\mathcal{E}
with
e
-plots given by

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)\,:=\,\mathrm{Span}_{\mathcal{O}(e)}\big\{\mathrm{d}v\,|\,v\in\mathcal{O}(e)\big\}\Big/\mathrm{d}\big(h(v^{1},\cdots,v^{q})\big)=\sum_{i}\frac{\partial h}{\partial z^{i}}(v^{1},\cdots,v^{q})\cdot\mathrm{d}v^{i}\,,

where
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}_{z}^{q})
ranges through any
smooth
function in
q
-variables, for any
q\in\mathbbm{N}
. Under any identification
\mathcal{O}(e)\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x})/I
, this can be equivalently expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)\,\cong\,\mathcal{O}\big(e\big)\cdot\big(\mathrm{d}x^{1},\cdots,\mathrm{d}x^{k}\big)\,\big/\,\mathrm{d}I\,,

(121)

(ii)

Similarly, the
moduli space of
n
-forms

\mathbold{\Omega}^{n}
is the sheaf
\mathbold{\Omega}^{n}\in\mathcal{E}
with
e
-plots

\mathbold{\Omega}^{n}\big(e\big)\;:=\;\bigwedge^{n}_{\mathcal{O}\left(e\right)}\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}\big(e\big)\,.

It is immediately obvious that for
e=\mathbbm{R}^{k}
, this recovers the standard differential forms on
\mathbbm{R}^{k}

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,\cong\,\Omega^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\,,

while, by Hadamard’s Lemma
2.6
, for any infinitesimal point
\mathbbm{D}
, this recovers the algebraic “Kähler” differential forms of the underlying
\mathbbm{R}
-algebra
47
47

47

Namely, by Hadamard’s lemma one can check that the quotient relation is equivalent to that of quotienting only by polynomials.
from (
13
).
More generally, by the partial Hadamard’s Lemma
4.1
, one sees similarly that this definition reproduces

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}\otimes\mathbbm{D})\;\cong\;\Omega^{1}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathcal{O}(\mathbbm{D})\oplus C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k})\otimes\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(\mathbbm{D})

on any thickened Cartesian space
\mathbbm{R}^{k}\times\mathbbm{D}
. The following immediate result justifies this definition on such more general thickened smooth probe spaces
e\in E
.

Lemma 4.41

(
1-forms inject into tangent algebras).

There is a canonical injection

\displaystyle\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)\;\lhook\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\;\mathcal{O}(Te)\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,

of
1
-forms on
e\in E
into the (smooth) functions on
Te\in E
, whose image is the fiber-wise linear maps internal to
\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

This follows similarly to the corresponding part of Lem.
4.38
, but now noting that
Te
is representable in the new topos. More explicitly, the first inclusion is immediately obvious under any representation
\mathcal{O}(e)\cong C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}^{k}_{x})/I
and the induced ones for
\mathcal{O}(Te)
(
120
) and
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)
(
121
), whereby it is identified with the mapping

f_{i}(x)\cdot\mathrm{d}x^{i}\longmapsto f_{i}(x)\cdot y^{i}\,.

Note that the inclusion is independent of the quotient representative when both sides are pulled back to
\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)
and
\mathcal{O}(Te)
.
The latter isomorphism above follows since in any site
E
consisting of duals of finitely generated algebras one has

\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{C^{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\big(C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R}),\,\mathcal{O}(Te)\big)\,\cong\,\mathcal{O}(Te)\,.

This justifies thinking of
1
-forms on
e
as the subset of
smooth and fiber-wise linear
functions
Te\rightarrow e
in the site
E
, since the inclusion maps onto elements linear in “the coordinate along the fibers”
f_{i}(x)\cdot y^{i}\in\mathcal{O}(Te)
from (
120
). That is,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{E}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,:=\,\mathrm{Im}\big(\Omega^{1}(e)\big)\;\;\subset\;\;\mathcal{O}(Te)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{E}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,.

(122)

Indeed, under the Yoneda embedding, the above image is identified with those maps that are
internally fiber-wise linear
, so that

\mathbold{\Omega}^{1}(e)\,\cong\,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{fib.lin.}}(Te,\,\mathbbm{R})\,.

∎

Since the fully classifying space of differential forms may be defined in this larger sheaf topos precisely as in Def.
4.33
, the following is immediate from Lem.
4.41
.

Corollary 4.42

(
Moduli spaces are isomorphic).

In a larger sheaf topos where tangent bundles are representable, the algebraic moduli space is canonically isomorphic to the corresponding fully classifying moduli space

\mathbold{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\,\cong\,\widehat{\mathbold{\Omega}}^{1}\,.
